Chapter 211: Divine Power

The old man was currently in Tingqiu Mountain. At this moment, he had already used all of his skills in order to escape, including the Hidden Earth Escape Talisman to get into the ground and through the mountain, then the Life-Saving Substitution Talisman — which was definitely a peerless treasure — to block the fatal blow. He had even used the Mountain God’s stone.

Even so, the old man was still uneasy. It was fortunate that he had activated the Life-Saving Substitution Talisman without hesitation the moment he escaped. If he had wanted to save time, it would have been too late to activate it when the spiritual sword was unsheathed. Even for a cultivator, how could his reaction speed possibly be faster than the sword light of an immortal sword?

But even though he had used the Life-Saving Substitution Talisman talisman so decisively, the old man had not escaped unscathed. The moment the immortal sword flashed past, the old man, who should have escaped entirely without injury, had also been captured by the sword light. The pain he felt was as though the talisman had not substituted his life at all, but as if he had been directly executed. A piece of his mental strength had been sliced off, and for a moment the old man truly thought he was dead.

Just a fleeting glimpse of this experience made the old man deeply understand that, if he had truly been hit by the immortal sword, his body and soul would definitely have been destroyed under the control of the immortal sword’s sword intent and sword Qi. Any thoughts of his soul escaping while leaving his corpse behind were absolute delusions.

The old man was truly frightened by the power of the immortal sword. According to legend, the immortal weapons were all unpredictable and mysterious. The immortal sword, as a weapon designed for killing, was even more powerful than the others. He had never seen one before and had once entertained the thought of finding one to look at, but all thoughts of that were gone now!

“Mountain God, save me! Mountain God, save me! Please, Mountain God, I beg of you, save me!”

The old man continued chanting, his voice growing more and more impatient. Despite being an immortal cultivator, he could barely control his physical condition, and a cold sweat broke out all over his body.

At the same time, he continued guiding his body to escape through the earth, and ran deeper into the depths of the treacherous land, drilling into the belly of those high mountains and peaks, even if only for a little more psychological comfort.

However, the old man was no true land god in the end. Even if he had a Hidden Earth Escape Talisman, the deeper he ran, the more spiritual energy he consumed.

‘Why haven’t you arrived yet? Why haven’t you arrived yet? I don’t have a second Life-Saving Substitution Talisman!’

The old man now had an epiphany: even treasures like the Life-Saving Substitution Talisman had their limits. He had managed to escape with his life the first time, but his mind had still been crippled. If he had a second talisman, even if he could truly “substitute for his life,” he would still certainly perish. The talisman worked by binding both his mind and his soul at the critical moment; his mind would probably be completely destroyed, which would be no different from a direct violent death.

Ji Yuan was flying in the sky with his supernatural eyes wide open, investigating the energy of the surrounding mountains. In addition to the snow-capped peaks, the mountains and surrounding land gave off an extraordinary charm and aura. This unknown land was obviously quite majestic, and was likely one of the border markers between Dazhen and the northern country of Tingliang.1

After a moment, Ji Yuan finally discovered the old man’s faint aura. He was trying his best to escape through the thick mountainous terrain and its rich soil. He clearly thought that he could use this to cover his tracks. The method he had used to escape was obviously no ordinary technique.

However, since he had been able to withstand a strike from the Green Vine Sword, and had even cut off his connection to his Qi, Ji Yuan did not dare to underestimate the old man.

The point of the Green Vine Sword rang out, and all the wind and snow within a ten zhang radius around the flying Immortal Sword shattered. The very next moment, the entire body of the sword trembled.

“Shiiiing…”

The sword rang out. It was nearly halfway out of its sheath, a full foot and six inches out, six inches further than it had been during its previous strike. A streak of silver light fell once more.

The old man, who was escaping deep into the belly of the mountain, suddenly felt the warning signs in his senses reach their peak, and a feeling of impending doom overwhelmed him.

‘My life is over! ’

As soon as this thought arose, something unexpected happened.

“Boom…”

The center of the mountain above the old man’s head suddenly exploded, and a giant hand made of rock stretched out from the belly of the mountain. It swung its arm right in the middle of the falling beam of sword light, and a powerful spiritual energy and divine light burst out in a clash.

“Bang…Boom…”

The old man was dumbstruck as he perceived the changes in the energy above him. It was as if he could see countless boulders, pieces of earth, and vegetation exploding and flying between the falling snow and wind in the sky. Gravel and mud rained down. A huge shadow pushed against the outside air, pressing down with each breath.

“Whoosh, whoosh…”

“Bang! Boom, crrrrk… crrrrkk…”

The giant arm made of mountains, mud, and boulders could not withstand the sword light. It broke apart, smashing into a small mountain nearby. For a moment, the very earth shook. The snow on the surrounding mountains exploded, sending avalanches everywhere, and the sky was filled with snow mist and dust.

“Boom…”

A towering, enormous shadow rose up from the mountain amidst the snow and fog. The broken arm and its countless boulders and rubble flew up again and merged back into the giant’s shadow. Its body was filled with a strong divine light; this was not the light of incense power, but rather the light of an orthodox Land God.

The agony of the strike just now made the Mountain God roar in anger, his voice as powerful and resonant as a large bell.

“I! Am! The God of Tingqiu Mountain!2 How dare you, evildoer…”

“Buzz…”

On the scabbard of the Green Vine Sword, the hidden word “conceal” faded out of view, while the word “edge” began to glow.3 Sword intent was released alongside the humming of the blade. All of the snow and wind in the sky shattered, and the sky cleared. Even if new snow fell from the sky, it soon melted under the endless sharpness.

An endless cold front, immeasurably colder than the harsh winter, rended the sky.

The Mountain God froze, and couldn’t utter the second half of his sentence. His huge body, as tall as the lower mountain peaks, bore the weight of the surrounding avalanches. He lowered his head and looked down at the tiny old man down below. Although he knew that this guy must have encountered a major crisis to have used the mountain god stone, this was a bit exaggerated.

He couldn’t help but whisper into the old man’s ears.

“What the hell kind of f***er did you provoke!?”4

Only then did the old man return to his senses. He took stock of his body from top to bottom, and didn’t find any wounds. He confirmed that he was not dead, and hurriedly bowed with cupped hands to the towering Mountain God, and shouted for help.

“Mountain God, please save my life at this time! No matter what, I must survive!”

As for his question of who Ji Yuan was, the old man didn’t know either.

Now, the sword was hanging over his head, and the confrontation was simply a matter of retaliation. It wouldn’t be good to say too much nonsense. Judging from the sword strike just now, the opponent was definitely aiming for his life.

The Mountain God was dozens of zhang tall, and his body was made of rocks and soil, as if he were a mountain in and of himself. He raised his head slightly and looked at the white-robed immortal cultivator riding a cloud a hundred zhang away. His loud bell-like sound resounded once more through this mountain region.

“Immortal Master, I am the Mountain God of Tingqiu Mountain. I have some history with the Immortal Master Li below. Could you…”

“I can!”

Ji Yuan stood atop the clouds, looking at the towering, burly mountain god with a pair of pale eyes. Although this was the first time he had seen this kind of mountain god, and he felt faintly shocked, he did not show it on his face.

The Mountain God was stunned when he heard Ji Yuan’s calm tone. However, before the Mountain God and the old man below could rejoice, Ji Yuan lightly uttered the following sentence.

“I might be able to set aside questioning why a righteous Mountain God like yourself has a past connection with this evil demon, but this evil cultivator has committed countless atrocities. He even dared to dabble in such evil arts as Ghost Mother of Nine Children. We must not let him get away with it today!”

As he spoke, Ji Yuan flew up higher in the clouds, rising above the Green Vine Sword. He pointed the down down at him, spinning his sword fingers in a circle.

It seemed like a threat, but in truth it was also a deliberate attempt to gain some distance. The power of a Mountain God of this level was nothing to scoff at, and Ji Yuan did not dare get too close.

At the same time, the sword energy of the Green Vine Sword grew stronger and stronger. Combined with the melting of the snow and cloud front above, the deep sword intent clearly transmitted the intent to kill.

Ji Yuan raised his body and flicked his sword fingers down. At the very moment the power of the Immortal Sword was drawn downwards, there came the feeling that the snow-white sky above was being dragged down alongside the green-tinged sword intent. The three elements — white above, green in the middle, and clear below — actually blended for an instant, forming a subtle feeling, as if the sword intent carried the power of the whole of the heavens.

It felt like the advent of some natural inspiration. Upon becoming aware of this subtle change, Ji Yuan decisively reversed the will of heaven and earth, and filled the air with the power of the heavens in his inner world. This crystallized intent and aura of influence overlapped and stabilized in between the imaginary and the real. The immortal sword hung in the air as if carrying the power of heaven, creating infinite pressure on the soul.

The sword had not yet been unsheathed, but it already had power comparable to the sky falling.

‘One sword suspended midair, with power overturning the sky!’5

This thought rose in the hearts of Ji Yuan, the Mountain God, and the evil old man at the same time.

The only difference between them was that Ji Yuan was deliberately experiencing and intensifying this spiritual pressure based on his intuition, while the latter only had increasingly intense intuitive sensations.

The power of immortal swords was undeniably very strong, but although it was quite powerful, there wouldn’t be this exaggerated, oppressive feeling of the sky falling.

The Mountain God of Tingqiu Mountain was the strongest opponent that Ji Yuan had faced head-on thus far.

Ji Yuan knew his own limits. He had outstanding advantages: his immortal sword was unparalleled in its power, and his cultivation practice was also progressing rapidly. He possessed extraordinary techniques, such as the True Fire of Samadhi, his varied and extended use of the sound of Imperial Edicts, and other supernatural immobilization methods. He had also studied dimensional storage techniques and the art of transformation. In addition, he had an immaculate body, and a supernatural eye that could see through Qi. he could reverse the destiny of heaven and earth, and manifest his inner world outside of his body to a certain extent.

However, he was also quite clear on his shortcomings. After all, he had only been cultivating for a short period of time. Apart from the Green Vine Sword, his other advantages, including his cultivation, had great potential but insufficient foundation. He was quite good at bluffing, but if he truly engaged in a life and death battle with the Tingqiu Mountain God, Ji Yuan was not certain whether his small body would be able to withstand it, nor did he want to test it out. After all, the opponent’s spiritual powers did not seem like something the immortal sword could destroy.

As for his Spirit Binding technique, Ji Yuan didn’t dare to use it now. Besides, the Tingqiu Mountain God didn’t look like he could easily be restrained by his own spiritual power.

From the previous change in the Mountain God’s tone, Ji Yuan had already concluded that the other party was a little scared, so he acted a little tougher and used the power of the immortal sword to cover up the fact that the distance between them was increasing.

However, he hadn’t expected that his unintentional action would lead him to comprehend a new application of his “influence.”6 It was truly a miraculous feeling, as if he had been gifted with divine power.

‘This is a sword that strikes down evil hearts!’

His enlightenment rose. Ji Yuan’s biggest wish at this moment was to stabilize this state. After dealing with this evil cultivator, he would immediately go thoroughly appreciate the opportunity he had been given by the Tao at this moment.

This kind of mentality was naturally reflected in his outward appearance, making Ji Yuan seem quite careless, as if the Mountain God and the evil cultivator under the power of the Green Vine Sword were not even worth paying attention to.

As the one who directly felt the sudden change in this situation, the Mountain God of Tingqiu Mountain was under unimaginable pressure. Th old man was pinned to the ground by the heavy weight of the pressure in his mind, and he couldn’t even stand up straight.

Chapter 212: Why Would I Have Bothered?

To be honest, the Mountain God was no longer thinking about how to protect the terrified evil cultivator below him who was unable to move. Instead, he was just trying to figure out how to escape.

Although the white-robed immortal said that he would leave him and his affairs alone, the Mountain God had just proclaimed that he “had a previous connection” with the old man. Thus, the immortal cultivator in white believed that this righteous Mountain God indeed had some past relationship with this evil demon.

What was the term for this situation? If the public of Ji Yuan’s previous life were asked, many people would jump out and inform the Mountain God that this was called “courting death”!

Although “death” did not exactly apply to the Mountain God per se, he indeed could appreciate its meaning and implications.

However, now that he understood, he had a feeling that it was too late. If the sword hanging in the air fell, it wouldn’t just be a matter of shedding a few layers of skin. His true body was also hidden inside the incarnation of the mountain.

Although the spiritual power and the divine light on this mountain did not diminish, both sides could easily sense the decline in the other’s momentum as they were fighting.

Ji Yuan had a keen sense of perception, and his supernatural eyes were fully opened, so he almost immediately sensed that the Mountain God’s influence was not as strong as before. This feeling made Ji Yuan immediately collect his thoughts. Now was not the time to chase after enlightenment.

Seeing that the Mountain God hesitated to speak, then looking at the limp old man down below, it seemed that the evil-piercing sword1 had achieved its desired effect. In addition, the sword force had consumed a lot of mental power, and was not easy to maintain. Ji Yuan immediately withdrew his inner world manifestation.

As soon as the inner world had closed, the feeling of the confluence of heaven and the sword’s power disappeared in an instant. Although the Green Vine Sword still carried the force of its sword intent and unparalleled power, it no longer made the Mountain God fear the terror of the sky falling and the earth collapsing from under him.

“The Tingqiu Mountain range is a vast area, with tall peaks and deep forests. You have achieved great success in your cultivation by becoming the true God of Tingqiu Mountain. As a righteous god, you should not have a close relationship with such evil individuals. If you have made any promises to him, the fact that you have not retreated even a single step under the might of my Heaven-Rending Sword2 means that you have lived up to that promise!”

Amidst the faint buzzing sounds of the blade, Ji Yuan reached out and grabbed the hilt of the Green Vine Sword, and pointed at a diagonal directly at the evil cultivator on the ground, his pale, sightless eyes turned towards the enormous eye sockets on the head of the Mountain God of Tingqiu Mountain.

“Will the Mountain God continue to stand in front of this evil cultivator, who deserves a fate worse than death?”

Ji Yuan’s words alleviated the Mountain God’s fears — his words implied that he could have a clear conscience even if he retreated now.

The Mountain God of Tingqiu Mountain breathed a sigh of relief. It was obvious that the other party had given him a way out. If he continued to resist, he would simply be too oblivious for his own good. After seeing the power of the Heaven-Rending Sword, he naturally understood that any fight would most likely result in serious injury to his body, or perhaps lead to more serious consequences.

As if realizing that his situation was getting worse, the old man on the ground came back to his senses from his dazed state, and repeatedly pleaded with the Mountain God.

“Mountain God Hong,3 please, you can’t just stand by and watch me die. Mountain God Hong, you promised you would come to help me when your stone was broken. You promised!”

The Mountain God’s enormous head turned down to look at the old man. His voice was deep and resonant as a bell.

“If it weren’t for me, you would have already died from that first sword strike. I didn’t leave, even under the pressure of that power just now. Isn’t that enough help? I have done my best. Do you really want me to die for you?”

The Mountain God raised his head to face Ji Yuan, who was nothing but a small figure in the distant clouds. He swung his enormous arms, leaving gusts of wind whistling behind them. Ji Yuan was afraid, but didn’t move at all from his position in the air. Sure enough, he watched as the mountain god’s arms collided.

“Boom…”

Dust was carried away by the impact, and the giant arms formed a gesture of a bow with cupped hands.

“I am the humble Hong Shengting, Mountain God of Tingqiu Mountain. Sir, it seems I have offended you just now. Please, I hope you can forgive me!”

Ji Yuan bowed with cupped hands to the Mountain God in return. He hesitated for a moment, but finally decided not to introduce himself. Although it was a little impolite, he really didn’t want to do so at the moment because there seemed to be some hidden Qi in the distance surrounding them.

After greeting Ji Yuan, the Mountain God took one step forward and began to walk with his huge body.

“Boom…boom…boom…”

The surrounding mountains shook. After only three steps, he had already put a considerable distance between himself and the old man Laxie Xiu. During this process, the old man seemed a little hysterical.

“Mountain God Hong! Hong Shengting! You can’t leave, you…”

Shiiing…

The immortal sword flashed with a ringing sound, and the old man was directly slashed by the silver sword light. The ringing abruptly stopped, but his body appeared to be unharmed. Instead, he collapsed to the ground, eyes closed.

Ji Yuan casually slid the sword back into the scabbard floating beside him, and in mere moments the sword intent in the sky disappeared without a trace.

The wind and snow began to fall from the sky once more with intermittent whooshing, and the Tingqiu Mountain Range was once more covered in heavy snow.

This type of sword strike was the same as the one used after the old man had escaped with his life-saving talisman, and had been discovered by Ji Yuan. It only destroyed most of his mind, but did not touch his body or soul. However, compared to the advantages he had gained from observing and developing the Heaven-Rending Sword, this development was insignificant.

This time, Ji Yuan personally held the hilt of the sword and drew it to strike. He used his own spiritual energy to mobilize the might of the Green Vine Sword, slashing away the majority of the evil cultivator’s mind, leaving only a trace of immortality.

Only Ji Yuan, with his supernatural eyes fully open, could grasp the subtleties of this sword-drawing technique. It was different from the ordinary method: the sword Qi was supplemented by sword intent, and how own spiritual energy drove the flow of the Qi. One step further was the destruction of the soul. The immortal sword could not do this by itself for the time being. This style was a consequence of having split the land apart.

With his mind and spirit exhausted, the old man couldn’t move at all, and his entire body was out of his control. He was unable to concentrate or use his spiritual energy. He fell into a complete coma state, which was rare for cultivators of his level.

The Mountain God looked at Ji Yuan, and then at the old man on the ground, and knew that the latter was not yet dead. However, he could not and did not want to care what would happen afterwards. He was even more unwilling to stay here and be further embarrassed, so he he took heavy steps away, shaking the mountains. “Bang”, “Bang”, “Bang”… He stepped deep into the wind and snow. After a few dozen steps, he had moved further away and onto another peak. The enormous mountain-made body slowly sat down, then leaned against the mountain peak.

Rumble… rumble… rumble…

As the mountains shook, the majority of the Mountain God’s incarnation sank into the surrounding surrounding mountains, leaving only a few large boulders exposed. It looked exactly like an ordinary rugged mountain. Even with Ji Yuan’s eyes, he couldn’t detect anything special about this mountain’s aura from afar, as if it were truly an ordinary landmark.

‘This is obviously a consequence of his mountain domain. The Mountain God of Tingqiu Mountain is no ordinary individual! ’

Ji Yuan still stood in the air at this moment, his supernatural eyes wide, scanning around Tingqiu Mountain. He see demonic Qi and some other special auras escaping into the distance, but he had no intention of investigating or pursuing them.

He figured that, after experiencing the might and intimidating power of the Heaven-Rending Sword just now, no one would be so blind as to dare approach him.

Since the rumors of Tianji Pavilion had spread, there were likely countless individuals paying attention to Dazhen, particularly its northern border.

After all, although Dazhen had countries neighboring it on many sides, it was generally considered to be located in the southern corner of the Eastern Cloud Continent. The north was a cold and bitter place in Dazhen, but with regards to the geography of the Eastern Cloud Continent where it was located, the north was actually more towards the center of the landmass.

‘There may be more villains like this old man who dare use evil methods to defy fate. This might be a good opportunity to give them a scare!’

With this thought in mind, Ji Yuan descended from the clouds, riding the wind, before finally landing beside the old man.

As a result of the battle just now, not only did a mountain collapse, but there were also more frequent avalanches. The old man lay in the heavy snow; as the Mountain God’s enormous body moved, it had shaken the snow loose and buried him.

Given the old man’s current condition, if nothing were to be done, he would soon be frozen into an ice sculpture. He most likely wouldn’t die, but he would probably have to wait to thaw out before waking up.

Just to be safe, Ji Yuan squatted down and used his martial arts skills to change the old man’s internal energy by sealing all the major acupoints in his body.

Just as he was about to stand up, a thought came to mind, and he approached the old man once more. He shaped the snow into the character “freeze” with his sword fingers, then pushed it into the old man’s body. Then he clapped his hands and stood back up.

After completing all of these tasks, Ji Yuan had no intention to leave directly. Instead, be bowed again in the direction that the Mountain God had left.

“Please, let us speak again, Mountain God.”

There was no response for a long time. He couldn’t be sure whether the true body of the Mountain God had already escaped to another place in the mountain range. Ji Yuan figured that this was most likely not the case; it was probably just that he didn’t want to come out again.

After a brief moment of contemplation, Ji Yuan decided to lift his foot and stomp lightly on the ground, using a subtle spiritual power to perform a very mild execution of the Spirit-Binding Technique, just enough for results.

“I invite Hong Shengting, the Mountain God of Tingqiu Mountain, to come and have a chat!”

The ripples of spiritual energy under his feet were weak and fluctuating, and were sensed by the Mountain God in an instant. However, Ji Yuan hadn’t made it too strong, which could be regarded as him affording the Mountain God some measure of respect.

A faint yellow light surrounded Ji Yuan. A rock broke through the mountain soil and drilled out of the snow. It appeared in front of him, and transformed into a man with rocky outgrowths and patterns on his face. He wore a stone-gray robe, and couldn’t hide the look of surprise on his face.

“Hong Shengting greets the Lord Immortal!”

The Mountain God looked at Ji Yuan with a complicated expression and bowed.

“If you had shown me this skill earlier, why would I have gone through all this trouble…”

天 (tiān): sky/heaven/day, 倾 (qīng): to overturn/to collapse, 剑 (jiàn): sword/sword strikes. Note that I translated it as “sword,” but it’s in the sense of “swordsmanship” or “style” rather than the object itself. Not so much a new title for the sword as a name of the technique.

洪 (hóng) could be his surname, but could also mean “Great,” as in Great Mountain God. Based on the sentences later on, it seems to be his name.

Chapter 213: Have A Good Night’s Sleep

However, when the Mountain God bowed with cupped hands in greeting Ji Yuan decided not to take advantage of his advantageous position: he also bowed with cupped hands in return at the same time. After the Mountain God had finished speaking, he immediately replied.

“Please don’t blame me, Mountain God Hong. I couldn’t call you, so I had to use this method to invite you here as a last resort!”

Ji Yuan can only try to make his tone seem sincere. After all, the current situation was rather awkward, and it would be easy for the Mountain God to be left with a bad impression that he was settling the score.

Without waiting for the Mountain God of Tingqiu Mountain to overthink it, Ji Yuan continued to speak.

“Does Mountain God Hong know the rumors about Tianji Pavilion’s divination?”

The Mountain God Hong Shengting glanced at the evil old cultivator on the ground and answered truthfully.

“Of course I know of it. Does the Lord Immortal believe this person came here due to that rumor?”

Ji Yuan thought it over for a moment.

“That may not necessarily be the case. Dazhen is a peaceful country; even if there are demonic cultivators who are plotting to usurp this rumored opportunity, they would do their best to follow along the path designated by fate. If such people wished to practice evil techniques like the Ghost Mother of Nine Children, they would only bring calamity upon themselves if they aren’t careful.”

The Mountain God couldn’t help but glance at this immortal cultivator in front of him, taking in the fact that he seemed to be blind. All he could do was think to himself: ‘Lord Immortal, it was you who became his calamity.’

Ji Yuan also glanced at the man on the ground, and continued after a brief pause.

“Judging from this person’s condition, he may not know all that much about the rumors of Tianji Pavilion. Instead, don’t you think he may have been used as a pawn by others, in an attempt to test us with evil methods?”

The Mountain God frowned. Was the man on the ground truly his old acquaintance? The man had once lived in Tingqiu Mountain for over a decade, and had given him much help. Otherwise, he wouldn’t have possessed this Mountain God Stone.

However, upon returning this final time, he had actually found a place with demonic miasma to practice, and cultivated evil techniques. The inconsistency was quite strange.

Of course, from the perspective of land gods like the Mountain God of Tingqiu Mountain, who were hardly ever worshipped and barely received any incense power, they didn’t care what kind of methods people cultivated, so long as they didn’t disturb his Tingqiu Mountain. In any case, every supernatural technique had its own method, perhaps it was just something different.

If the Lord Immortal had summoned him at this time to question him, then the Mountain God would naturally reveal all of these considerations to him, but since he hadn’t asked, he wouldn’t risk appearing hypocritical and mentioning them.

With these thoughts in mind, the Mountain God could hardly wait to hear his next words, so he went ahead and asked.

“Then, Lord Immortal, you have summoned me here, is there anything you wish to ask of me?”

Ji Yuan bowed again to the Mountain God.

“I do have a presumptuous request…”

Ji Yuan accepted his bow in return1 and looked to the north, where the Tingqiu Mountain range stretched on endlessly.

“At this time, the rumors of Tianji Pavilion are still unclear, but many people are concerned about them. If they just wanted to take a look at Dazhen, it would be fine. But it’s disgusting to see this rat-like person under your feet.”

In truth, the man on the ground was was quite skilled in cultivation. Without the Green Vine Sword, Ji Yuan would not have dared to confront him head-on. Nonetheless, it was no surprise for Ji Yuan to belittle him as a rodent.2

Ji Yuan paused for a while before continuing.

“Tingqiu Mountain is located on the northern border of Dazhen. It is an insurmountable natural barrier for mortals, and it would take a long time for cultivators to cross. It would be best if Mountain God Hong could pay attention to it, and even give a reminder to those individuals who are not on the right path.”

The Mountain God looked to the north thoughtfully.

“What do you mean, Immortal? After the power of the Immortal’s Heaven-Rending Sword today, would there still be such foolish people as the man under your feet?”

The Mountain God now assumed that the immortal had more or less intended to deter the individuals with evil intent with his display just now. As a mountain god, it was naturally impossible for him to fail to notice the Qi in the distance just now.

“Haha, the Mountain God’s words are indeed one possibility. For example, a True Demon who had suffered a great loss at my hands and the hands of the Dragon Lord of the Tongtian River would not dare show up again in Dazhen, but given his temperament, he must be extremely antagonistic. Even this evil cultivator may have the shadow of his actions behind him.”

His words left a lot to the imagination, but Ji Yuan stopped there.

The pregnant woman who was neither human nor ghost was probably a native of Dazhen. In this manner, she had cultivated the demonic ghost path at the border of Dazhen, and when she had grown strong enough, she hid in the far reaches of the country’s wilderness, so that she could act how she pleased.

This was indeed one way to get around the blood oath. However, the timing was off, as the pregnant woman had a total of seven ghost children, with another in her belly. It wasn’t possible to have achieved this level of strength in just a few years. It was only in recent years that someone had been discovered with their heart dug out and eaten, causing mass panic.

After much deliberation, it might still have been a coincidence. The old man received the evil method, and chose a favorable location to refine it. In the second half of this year, he seemed to be a little impatient due to various happenings — or perhaps the pregnant woman had a bad temperament and something went wrong, leading to the series of incidents in which Du Heng and others were ambushed in the north, followed by Ji Yuan’s arrival.

However, Ji Yuan merely considered these points to himself, and did not elaborate to the Mountain God in detail. Therefore, the Mountain God of Tingqiu Mountain was still thinking about the “truth of the Tao hidden behind the conflicts,” following the meaning indicated by Ji Yuan’s words.

The Mountain God thought about it for a while, but he still accepted it. The current situation was stronger than could be dealt with by individuals alone.

“Since the Lord Immortal has entrusted me with this task, I will sleep less and pay more attention to it.”

This kind of thing was just a verbal agreement. Ji Yuan did not truly expect this Mountain God, who utterly lacked interest in the affairs of the world outside his mountain range, to work hard, but at least it would have some small effect.

Afterwards, Ji Yuan chatted with the Mountain God a bit more about the evil old man, and then they left separately. One fled back into the mountains, while the other picked up the unconscious demonic cultivator and the pregnant woman still under the immobilization technique, and brought them to Tingshui county.

. . .

In the inn in Tingshui County, all the men and warriors were a little anxious. Even Du Heng couldn’t help but worry.

Several days had passed, and Mr. Ji had not returned yet. He had been chasing such a strange, evil creature, and he was afraid that some unexpected calamity might occur.3

The broken roof and walls of the inn had been repaired with the help of the warriors on a day when it hadn’t snowed.

The government of Tingshui County had also sent people to look at the murder case at the inn. Although the inn servant’s account made several of the constables terrified, they could only identify it as a bandit.4 At most, they would only make a note: the inn found someone they suspected was a demon.

Since several of the warriors said that the other party had been beaten back, while the others said it had been chased away, the constables did not want to move about in the winter. They didn’t want to stay in this unlucky inn, either, so they had already retreated. Now the entire inn consisted of its original staff, minus the one unlucky servant.

It was nighttime. Du Heng and the others, as well as the original innkeeper and servant, were eating at the tables in the lobby on the first floor. During this period of time, everyone ate and lodged right next to each other; no one wanted to be alone.

Strictly speaking, it was only evening, but it grew dark quickly along the northern border in this season. With the heavy snow, it was almost pitch black outside.

The people at the table ate absent-mindedly. The howling of the blizzard outside continued. It was likely that it would continue all night.

“Knock, knock, knock…”

The knock on the door startled everyone. Several of the warriors couldn’t help but grab their weapons.

“Young Master Du, it’s Mr. Ji, I’m back!”

Ji Yuan’s peaceful voice sounded. Many people in the inn felt relieved, but they remained vigilant.

“Brother Li, let’s open the door!”

Li Tongzhou nodded. He left the seat alongside Du Heng, walked to the door, and carefully pulled open the bolts. The door of the inn was immediately blown open by the force of the wind and snow.

“Whooooo… whooooooo…”

The strong wind blew in, carrying snowflakes inside. Ji Yuan still stood at the door in an elegant white robe. Two people were bound by some kind of transparent silk thread, lying to the left and right of his feet. One of them was the woman, who seemed to be neither human nor ghost.

“Mr. Ji! Are you alright? Come in and warm up!”

Ji Yuan waved his hand.

“I won’t come inside. I just came here to let you see these two demons, so you can rest at ease. You all recognize this woman, the one next to her is her master. There was no one else besides them.”

At this point, Ji Yuan glanced at the dishes in the hall and sniffed his nose.

Du Heng reacted quickly. Seeing that Mr. Ji did not intend to come in, he immediately ran back to the table, bringing back a jar of wine, and took out a roast chicken that no one had touched yet, alongside the plate.

“Mr. Ji, Tingshui County’s Blade Roast5 is not a famous wine, but it warms up the body. There is also this roast chicken, which we haven’t touched! “

The Wei family had sent a letter to Du Heng long ago, informing him that although Mr. Ji was not addicted to alcohol, he was actually a good drinker, and would not be too picky about wine.

Ji Yuan didn’t refuse. He smiled and directly held the plate with the roast chicken in one hand, and put the Blade Roast wine on the plate. If he changed his clothes, he would look like a waiter.

“Okay then. I will be taking my leave now. We will meet again if it is fated.”

As he spoke, Ji Yuan took something that looked like a green bamboo pole from beside the door. Then he casually picked up the two people on the ground, one with the big end and the other with the tapered end, and carried the whole on his shoulders. The thin bamboo rod that was being used as a pole bent greatly with the weight, but it did not break.

With one hand holding the plate of roast chicken and the wine jug, and the other holding the bamboo pole, Ji Yuan walked briskly into the night wind and snow, and quickly disappeared.

Even though it was cold at the door, the people who had gathered around waited for a long time without dispersing.

“A fishing rod!”

“Huh?”

“I said, it was a fishing rod, and those people were probably tied up with fishing line!”

Du Heng said these inexplicable words, then smiled at the others.

“It’s freezing, close the door. We’ll have a good night’s sleep tonight!”

At this point, the others returned to their senses and relaxed. As for such miraculous people like Mr. Ji traveling about on a snowy night, they obviously didn’t need to worry about it.

Their tense nerves eased, and the atmosphere in the hall changed from its recent gloomy state, becoming very warm…

T/n: they’re really just bowing back and forth

T/n: brief moment of silence for my friend’s pet rat, who is actually really really cute and we’re both sad that rats get such a bad rap

Lit: “three long and two short,” refers to an unexpected calamity or accident especially fatal ones. It’s theorized that it could refer to: 1) the coffin box, which is made of 2 long sides and 3 short sides; 2) incense divinations, where the most dangerous one is 3 long and 2 short (but it’s the opposite…); 3) a historical swordsman

T/n: LOL RIP, lady was casually destroyed by JY and then downgraded to bandit. Couldn’t be me XD But also, remember that in this world most people aren’t aware of the supernatural beyond widespread faith

A blade roast is the name of a cut of meat (from the shoulder of the cow), but I guess it’s also the name of this wine. A lot of translation confusion

Chapter 214: I’ll Confess Everything

Content warning: mild depictions of torture (punishment for demonic spirits). Starts from “During this whole process” until the end of the chapter – summary at the end of chapter.

There was only one inn in the small county of Tingshui, so the inn was also nicknamed Tingshui Inn.

At this time, in the lobby of Tingshui Inn, the group of warriors was in a relaxed mood, and returned to the questions they had been asking Du Heng before.

Li Tongzhou raised a cup of wine in a toast to Du Heng, and asked with some ulterior motives.

“Brother Du, who exactly is this Mr. Ji? We have been through many life and death crises, and have survived many difficulties together, we can be considered friends in adversity. Tell us about him!”

“Yes, Warrior Du!”

“Yeah, that’s right, Warrior Du, tell us about him. It was like this, like how Mr. Ji did it, hoooh…”

A man with a loud voice blew out a breath in an imitation of Ji Yuan’s previous action. It wasn’t quite right, but everyone knew what he meant by it.

“Just with a breath, he turned those terrifying ghost puppets into ashes. That ability is unbelievable! Is Mr. Ji an immortal?”

“Yes, yes! My father once told me, better for us warrior to believe in the blade in our hands than in ghosts and corrupt officials. I always thought those mysterious things were just legends. This time I realized there were worlds beyond what I knew!”

“That’s right, Old Cui, go to the back kitchen and cook some more dishes. I’ll serve you guys some more drinks.”

The innkeeper chimed in with the cheering.

Du Heng was also in high spirits. He couldn’t refuse, nor did he want to. This group of friends was very different from the friends he had gone to fight the tiger with on a whim. Although it hadn’t been very long, they had all traveled together for the past few years, fighting bandits, catching criminals, and even eliminating corrupt officials. This time, they had fought together against a ghost and some evil beings, and had never abandoned each other. They were now true life-and-death companions.1

Although he wouldn’t talk too much about Ji Yuan’s personal affairs, Du Heng felt that he should able to clarify some things that everyone already knew but hadn’t quite put together yet. Judging from Mr. Ji’s previous actions, he didn’t seem to hide things too much.

“Okay, I’ll tell you about him. Let me be clear in advance: some things when spoken may provoke the gentleman, so I won’t say much. If it’s something I can share, I will naturally do so!”

“Of course!”

“That’s what it should be!”

“Alright!”

“Hey, Mr. Du, tell us quickly, I’ll pour you a drink!”

……

“Ahem~”

Du Heng cleared his throat, then leaned forward and spoke.

“You all know that I sent a letter to the gentleman before winter arrived, informing him about the abnormal situation here, right?”

Everyone nodded. Even the inn staff who hadn’t known about that before subconsciously followed along.

“Then, tell me, how long does it take for a letter to travel from Jin Province to Ji Province, specifically Desheng Prefecture in Ji Province!”

“Ji Province?”

“My goodness, it would take several months!”

The shopkeeper of the inn interjected in an exaggerated manner.

“I once wrote to a merchant in the Capital Prefecture, and it took half a year to arrive!”

Du Heng smiled.

“So, I think that maybe my letter arrived in Ji Province a few days ago, and was seen by Mr. Ji.”

The loud man looked around and couldn’t help but interrupt.

“The journey is so long, but Mr. Ji arrived here in such a short time? He could only have flown over to get here so fast!”

“Ahem… you said it, not me!”

Du Heng coughed and answered mysteriously. Everyone around him suddenly realized. Combined with their experiences over the past few days, their guesses became much clearer in their hearts.

It was a bit that, although Du Heng spoke at length that night, he didn’t reveal the answer to questions of where Mr. Ji lived. The others did not push him.

. . .

The object of discussion, Ji Yuan, didn’t think much about the group behind him. He was ready to deal with this master and disciple pair of evil cultivators, and then concentrate on carefully comprehending the insights he had gained from the previous battles.

The fishing rod on Ji Yuan’s shoulder was actually nothing special. However, with his personality, he preferred to use old objects that he was accustomed to.

Thus, after making another fishing rod, he wrote a few words on it and used some spiritual techniques to make superficial changes to the fishing rod and line — that way, the fishing rod could be rolled up like a ball of wool and tucked into his sleeves.

The excuse he had used to let the Old Turtle watch over the fishing rod, that it was inconvenient to carry, was purely let the old turtle stay there.

At this moment, Ji Yuan was carrying these two evil individuals, He couldn’t return to Ning’an County; he was afraid of contaminating his own land. To deal with these two, he would have to leave it to professionals: professional ghosts and gods.

Jin Province was vast and sparsely populated, and many counties and districts did not have any decent City God Temples. Even some of the City Gods in the prefectural capitals did not reach so deep into the hinterland of Dazhen. Ji Yuan was not familiar with them, so his action of capturing these people up might lead to suspicion and accusations. It would be a long process to resolve the embarrassment, which would be a bit troublesome.

The first solution Ji Yuan thought of was the underworld of Chunhui Prefecture. After all, he had already experienced the interrogation of the serpent demon. From the information that the City God’s underworld officials had conveyed to him before, Ji Yuan recognized their professional ability.

So, after leaving Tingshui County, Ji Yuan directly jumped into the sky, riding the clouds and controlling the wind to disperse the surrounding air currents carrying snow, temporarily creating a windless environment for himself.

Ji Yuan looked at the roast chicken and wine jug in his hand. He had indeed not eaten or drank for several days.

On the plate in his right hand, the roast chicken, which had completely cooled by now, was slowly warmed up again. After a few breaths, it was steaming once more, with a fragrant aroma.

“Not bad, not bad. My heating technique is quite good, I didn’t burn it this time.”

As Ji Yuan was eating roast chicken and drinking Blade Roast wine on the road, people began spreading the news about the fight that happened in Tingqiu Mountain just earlier. To a certain extent, this was equivalent to a confirmation that there was a hidden master inside Dazhen.2

. . .

It was after sunset on the next day. It was already late at night outside of the City God Temple of Chunhui Prefecture. At this time of night, when no one was walking around aside from the night watchman and the night patrol, a gentleman in a white robe was carrying two people on either end of a bamboo pole that curved exaggeratedly under the weight, approaching the City God Temple. It was Ji Yuan, just returning from Jin Province.

When he had approached to a certain distance in front of the City God Temple, Ji Yuan used his spiritual energy to convert between Yin and Yang, and crossed a certain boundary between the Yin and Yang realms. The sight of the Ghost Gate appeared in front of him.

At the sight of this man in a white robe carrying two people over, several ghost messengers at the Ghost Gate stepped forward and asked.

“Who are you, and why have you invaded the underworld realm of Chunhui Prefecture?”

The visitor was definitely not a ghost. The ghost messenger could tell from his heavy footsteps and his internal Qi heat. However, he was obviously not a mortal either.

In order to save some trouble, Ji Yuan directly took out the yin wood plaque that he had retrieved from Hu Yun after returning to Ning’an, and handed it to the ghost messenger.

“This is the wooden plaque of the Lord City God of Chunhui Prefecture. Please inform him that Ji Yuan is visiting!”

As soon as the wooden sign was in his hand, the ghost messenger felt the majestic aura of his own Lord City God preserved within it. He did not dare to neglect his duties. He told Ji Yuan, “Sir, please wait a moment,” and hurriedly entered the underworld.

After the City God of Chunhui Prefecture saw the yin wood plaque, Ji Yuan didn’t encounter any more troubles. After greeting them and explaining the situation, the two demonic cultivators, the old man and the young woman, both had their souls directly extracted and locked in the depths of the torture prison.

During this whole process, the woman was still immobilized, and the old man was still in a coma.

About a day later, the old man’s soul finally woke up under the nourishment of Yin Qi in the underworld. The moment he regained consciousness, he felt the coldness around him, and could hear screams in the distance.

His vision and memory were a little blurry. He tried his best to recall what had happened just before. A master had come to chase him down, the Mountain God was defeated, and then he had no memory of what happened afterwards…

“Am I dead?”

“Haha, that’s right. You’re as good as dead now!”

A voice sounded in front of him, causing the old man to raise his head abruptly. His vision became clear. A tall figure in an official’s uniform with a terrifyingly ugly face stood in front of him. He looked around and saw torture instruments everywhere. Deformed monsters were chained to them all around him, some being whipped and screaming.

“Where? Hehe, he asked himself where he was!”

“Hahahahahaha…”

“Yeah, hehehehe…”

“Hohohoho…”

“Wooahahahaha…”

He was surrounded by sharp and terrifying laughter. Some of it came from the eccentric people who looked like officials, some from those monsters on the torture instruments, who seemed to be cultivators. The old man felt a wave of malicious energy wash over him.

“Hahahaha, aahhhh ahhhhh… Hahaha… Aaaaaahhh…”

A monster who had been laughing just moments before was immersed into a frying pan in the next moment. Alongside a sizzling sound, it immediately let out a shrill scream. But even as it screamed in pain, it still smiled directly at the old man.

All the evil spirits and monsters around him were laughing. They all knew that the old man had committed a grievous sin: he was locked on the copper pillar of the Evil Barrier Platform, and the evil black cloud of energy above his head was so thick. The ghosts and monsters in this hell wanted someone to be as miserable as them — preferably even worse.

The man in official uniform gazed at the old man with a stern expression.

“Where do you think you are?”

The old man’s head was numb. This was probably the legendary Underworld Refining Prison. Not even cultivators dared to come here.

The miserable laughter around the room brought great excitement. Many of the monsters that looked deformed now had actually once been human beings. They were tortured by the officials in the Refining Prison, and no longer held their human form. The old man had heard the rumors, so he immediately came to his conclusions.

“They are… why are they laughing at me?”

The old man asked, trembling.

“Hehe, naturally, it’s because they can see someone worse off than they are. You, an evil cultivator, have committed countless sins. After the immortal removed the talisman from your body, you were so clearly evil…”

The most vicious executioner in Chunhui Prefecture’s Punishment Division gave him a look and said.

“The Punishment Division doesn’t plan to interrogate you. The Master said that a demon like you wouldn’t confess anything. We will just perform thousands of capital punishments. Besides, your cultivator soul is quite solid, so there’s no need to worry about anything. We have a saying in our Punishment Division: ‘a quick death is bliss.’ Hehehehe…you’ll understand soon!”

As the official spoke, the Evil Barrier Platform seemed to be moving, and a dark red region appeared at the front. The wailing of countless long-tongues demons, the withered bones, and the dead souls were getting closer and closer…

Only half a day later, in the depths of the Punishment Division’s prison, the old man was left with only one sentence, aside from his hysterical screams.

“Aaaaaahhh… I’ll confess, I said I’ll confess everything, I’ll confess!”

Summary: JY brings both the old man and the woman to the Punishment Division of Chunhui Prefecture. The old man wakes up in a torture chamber and Does Not Have A Good Time. Catharsis is had.

T/n: ORV flashbacks T_T

Lit: rumors of a crouching tiger, hidden dragon

Chapter 215: A Long Dream

No matter how miserably the old man cried or begged, the torturer just carried out the execution without asking any questions. The innumerable demonic spirits around him laughed even more piercingly.

There was also a brief period where the torturers left the room. During this period of time, the incomplete demons and monsters limped, crawled, and dragged themselves to gather around the old man, desperately trying to tear at and devour him. This kind of deformed madness and greed was even more terrifying than the demons of the outside world, and his cultivator’s soul allowed him to feel every bit of pain without being fatally injured.

Once the torturer returned, these mutilated ghosts immediately switched from their vicious glee to panic, and fled one after the other. This cycle repeated, with each turn being slightly different, never to repeat. If it did repeat, it was simply because that method of punishment had caused the old man more pain and mental destruction than the others, so they chose to do it again.

Just above the bottom level of the Punishment Division’s prison, the Head Official of the Punishment Division and the Martial Judge of the Rewards and Punishments Divisions observed the fate of the evil cultivator through the gloomy mist, watching as he longed for relief and liberation.

“Humph. With such a twisted character, it’s no wonder he chose such an evil demonic path.”

The Martial Judge’s lips twitched. Even the other ghost officials in the underworld feared the Punishment Division’s lowest levels. Prisoners were subjected to the most horrific punishments, even for cultivator souls. How long could they endure it without breaking down?

“Is it time to interrogate him about the events in Jin Province?”

The Head Official of the Punishment Division smiled and shook his head.

“Don’t worry, there’s no rush. This man has a solid soul. His body was well-tempered with spiritual energy and power, and he has been practicing his cultivation for a long time. Let him suffer a little more. After a while, as long as the torturer intends to interrogate him, I guarantee that he will remember everything, down to the moment of his birth, and confess it all eagerly.”

“Hm!”

The Punishment Division had its own methods of interrogation, and had clearly determined its scale and intensity. The Martial Judge didn’t say anything more. This evil cultivator scum was not worthy of sympathy. He would truly be miserable when he had confessed everything he could, and then found out that he would continue to be punished.

As for the pregnant woman’s interrogation, it was much more straightforward. Aside from her good looks, she otherwise had the heart and temperament of a country woman in her entirety. Although she had practiced evil techniques and had eaten human hearts, she almost directly collapsed when she arrived in the underworld and saw the ghosts and demonic spirits there.

Someone like this would naturally not know all that much. Apart from the demonic methods taught to her by her master, which were said to enable one to become the Ghost Mother of Nine Children — a being who could fly into the sky, escape into the earth, devour gods, and destroy immortals — she didn’t know much else.

However, people in the underworld scoffed at her statement. Generally speaking, a fetus in the womb was considered to be alive after three months.1 Seeing that her belly was almost seven or eight months along, even the ghost baby in her womb was incomplete in spirit and body. This kind of exaggerated talk had no credibility.

. . .

For the time being, Ji Yuan didn’t care much about the things happening in the underworld of Chunhui Prefecture. If they couldn’t pry open the evil cultivator’s mouth and make him talk, then naturally he wouldn’t be able to either.

At this moment, Ji Yuan had already returned to Ju’an Pavilion in Ning’an County. When he arrived home, the brocade bag was still hanging on the door outside the main house. The courtyard was already covered in new layer of snow, and there were no traces of paw print tracks or footsteps. It seemed Hu Yun hadn’t been here recently.

It was now early in the morning. Ji Yuan picked the brocade bag back up, opened the door, walked to the wooden table in the room, and sat down. Then, he took out a few talismans and a yellowed book from his sleeves.

One of them was the Hidden Earth Escape Talisman, which still had some spiritual energy. There was also a Qi Concealing Talisman and a Heart Purification Talisman. These three talismans were obviously no ordinary goods, and the spiritual light around them was extraordinary.

Then again, talismans were considered a specialized branch of spiritual practice. It took a long time, and was slow in giving returns, so not many individuals practiced it intensively. However, those who had achieved success in the practice were very popular. After all, everyone liked spiritual talismans, so each individual’s technique could be considered a trade secret. Various kinds of talismans were kept secret and passed on by word of mouth; not even the Old Dragon was familiar with the specific varieties and intricacies of talismans.

Ji Yuan didn’t expect the evil cultivator to give him any surprises in this regard. The only thing that disappointed him was that the life-substitution talisman was gone.

The other elements of the talisman arts were just extensions of some supernatural techniques, but the Life-Saving Substitution Talisman was truly miraculous, and was likely quite costly to make.

The book itself was a completely ordinary book, with no supernatural energy left inside it. It was revealed to be the demonic cultivation method, called “The Great Method of Refining and Controlling the Ghost Mother.”

“Humph!”

Ji Yuan snorted coldly. Just from the name, he could tell that it contained similar kinds of twisted methods like refining corpses and raising ghosts. Even if it had succeeded, the woman would have been a victim in the end, not a legitimate disciple at all.

It was just that this kind of book only had pictures and text, with no spiritual meaning imbued in it. It was easy to make mistakes by just practicing it casually. The old man didn’t seem to have received any true teachings, which meant that the woman was indeed playing the role of an experimental subject.

After reading it over carefully, Ji Yuan became more and more irritated. If it weren’t for the fact that he was concerned about the kind of metamorphosis that would allow someone to survive after being decapitated, and wished to see if there was anything worth comprehending within the book, he would have destroyed it long ago.

Some of the contents were unbearably disgusting, but he forced himself to finish reading it. Suddenly, a fire burst out from Ji Yuan’s hands, startling the paper crane that had just poked its head out of his arms so much that it shrunk back into the bag.

“Just keeping this would also cause harm!”

In only half a breath of time, under the control of Ji Yuan’s fire, the entire book had been burned, breaking into fine powder and scattering out of the house in the breeze.

“Huh… This spiritual technique of mine is still the best. I’m going to go to sleep and practice dreaming cultivation!”

Ji Yuan didn’t know if anyone else could practice in their sleep like him, and he didn’t know how the efficiency of his method compared to meditation and enlightenment in the Holy Lands of various immortal sects, but just the ability to comprehend the supernatural in a dream state was definitely extraordinary.

He woke up from his first dream state on the second day because he had received news from the underworld of Chunhui Prefecture. The message had been passed on to the ghosts and gods in this region at the border of Desheng Prefecture, and on and on at the border of each county, until it finally reached Ji Yuan.

As expected, the old man had no connection to the True Demon, at least on the surface. However, that also meant that a very small group of people were getting curious about the rumors of Tianji Pavilion, and thought they could get past by being clever.

As for the fate of the evil cultivator, the underworld of Chunhui Prefecture didn’t elaborate, and Ji Yuan didn’t want to ask.

After learning the news, Ji Yuan sent the visiting ghost messenger away. He couldn’t wait to fall asleep again.

On this attempt, time passed even faster during his sleep. When no one came to disturb him for a long time, he actually ended up dreaming for four or five months, uninterrupted.

Those in the county who knew Ji Yuan thought that Mr. Ji might have gone on a long journey again, except the spiritual energy in the courtyard remained… until the next year, when the jujube tree was in full bloom and the fragrance of jujube flowers filled the air, bringing peace and tranquility.

The seasons had circled around back to spring. On this day, a red fox tried his best to avoid the crowds of people, cats, and dogs in the county city, and hurriedly crossed the streets, arriving at Ju’an Pavilion, one of the more secluded places on Tian Niu Lane.

The fox glanced at the jujube tree in the yard that was full of flowers, then jumped over the wall and landed in the courtyard.

This was already the tenth time at least that Hu Yun had come to Ju’an Pavilion since last winter. Each time, he failed to see Mr. Ji. However, this time was a little different. He sensed something as soon as he entered the courtyard and looked up at the branches of the jujube tree.

There was a paper crane on a branch that was actually imitating the bees nearby, poking at the tiny jujube flowers with its paper beak.

“Paper Crane, is Mr. Ji awake?”

Hu Yun was overjoyed when he saw the paper crane. He directly asked his question to the little paper crane, seeing that it looked like a spirit.

However, the latter’s intelligence was not high enough to be able to communicate smoothly with the fox. It just flew down upon hearing its master’s name, landing on the stone table, and looked at the red fox. Then, in the very next moment, it suddenly flapped its wings and flew to the main house, landing at the door. It shrunk itself down and quickly slipped in through the crack in the door.

“Hey…”

Hu Yun had just opened his mouth and uttered out a word when the paper crane disappeared.

After a few breaths, the sound of a yawn came from within the room.

When Hu Yun heard this sound, his first reaction was surprise, but guilt and even a little panic.

‘Oh no! If Lu Shanjun finds out that I woke up Mr. Ji, I’m dead meat! ’

Hu Yun’s next instinct was to run away: so, he took a running start and leaped onto the wall of the small pavilion, then jumped to the ground and fled Tian Niu Lane.

“Hoooh…aaah…”

Ji Yuan yawned and stretched, then sat up from the bed. Everything except the bed was covered in a layer of dust.

“This dream was quite long!”

He looked in the direction of the door again, as if he could see through it, and muttered.

“Is this fox messing with me now? Did he run away when I woke up?”

The paper crane flew into the brocade bag by his bedside. Ji Yuan had naturally not been awoken by the paper crane — instead, the crane had come inside specifically because he had sensed that his master was about to wake up.

“I can’t sleep anymore. If I go to sleep again, I will miss the birth of Master Yin’s second child. I wonder if Yin Qing has already gone to visit.”

T/n: I still have a soft spot in my heart for Ying Ruoli as my favorite character, but man that adorable paper crane is in second place and catching up fast…

Note that the debate over when a fetus is considered “alive” is a very controversial debate, and LOTS of people have different opinions on when, how, and what the boundary line is.

Chapter 216: A Desolate Waystation On a Rainy Night

Ji Yuan left the bed and sniffed at the fragrance of the flowers that floated in the courtyard. It was fresh and elegant. Although it was not particularly supernatural, it nonetheless could calm the mind and soothe the spirit. At the very least, it made the people of Tian Niu Lane sleep soundly at night, and Ji Yuan was no exception. Otherwise, how could he have had such a peaceful dream for the past six months? The first half of his dream had mainly been concerned with cultivating the mind, while the second half was so vague that he couldn’t be certain whether he had been cultivating.

However, he felt good after getting up, so it could be considered quite effective.

He rolled up his sleeves and took a look at his arms. Although he had lost a little weight, it wasn’t too exaggerated. Although the circulation of the five elements in his body was still nothing more than a trickle, they were spiritually perfect, balanced and endlessly regenerating. As he breathed spiritual energy in and out in his sleep, he could replenish the five element energy and nourish his whole body. At the very least, now he wouldn’t easily starve to death.

He tied his loose long hair into a bun, took the black jade hairpin from the head of his bed, and inserted it into the bun. Two locks of hair hung over his temples, giving the look its own charm. He mused that many people in his previous life didn’t understand the value in the craft of hairstyles.

If one took a closer look at this black jade hairpin, it would become clear that the jade had become much more transparent, and the quality had dramatically increased. Even the merchant who had sold it to Ji Yuan would likely not even recognize that this was once that inferior-quality jade hairpin.

Perhaps it was due to the influence of his Immaculate Body, or perhaps his Immaculate Body was conceived due to some fundamental change in himself; Ji Yuan didn’t think too much about it. He just focused on the advantage of being able to save the trouble of washing, and his belongings were also much stronger and more durable — his three sets of clothes, the white, gray, and blue robes, had never been torn.

After putting on his over-robe and taking out the brocade bag containing the paper crane and stuffing it in his sleeves, Ji Yuan walked to the door. The Green Vine Sword floated up and followed him.

He opened the door, and the bright sunlight came streaming in. He looked up at the flowering jujube tree outside. Swarms of bees flew among the branches, collecting the extraordinary pollen.

Just because Ji Yuan was able to eat less, didn’t mean that he disliked eating. Likewise, that didn’t mean that he wouldn’t feel hungry. Although the feeling of hunger could be weakened or even eliminated, eating was one Ji Yuan’s great pleasures in life, and he would not give it up.

For example, although the jujube honey brewed from the jujube tree in his own yard could not be said to be the best in the world, it definitely had a unique flavor. There were not many beekeepers near Ning’an County, and it was probably due to the wild bees.

“I wonder where the beehive is?”

Hearing this, the paper crane in the brocade bag in his sleeve poked its head out of the bag, tilting it to look up at its master. Ji Yuan looked down at it.

“Do you know?”

He subconsciously asked. The paper crane’s gestures increased in intensity, and with a few movements it skillfully crawled out of the brocade bag, spread its wings, and flew up into the sky.

“Hey, hey, hey! Stop! Stop!”

Ji Yuan looked at the little paper crane who was eagerly acting as his guide and didn’t know whether to laugh or cry.

“The jujube flowers have only just bloomed. It will take time to make honey. Let’s wait until next time!”

In truth, Ji Yuan could also find the hive by following the route the bees took back. He had just voiced this question casually.

The courtyard gate of Ju’an Pavilion had not been opened for half a year. People in the county probably thought that Ji Yuan was not home. Considering that the second baby of the Yin family was about to be born, Master Yin must certainly have written him a letter. That letter was most likely kept in the county’s government office.

Ji Yuan went straight to pay a visit to the county government office. As he expected, he found three letters waiting for him there: two were from Yin Qing, and one was from Yin Zhaoxian.

After having a meal outside and returning home, Ji Yuan sat in the courtyard of Ju’an Pavilion and opened the three letters.

The former’s first letter not only talked about university life, but also mentioned his unborn sibling in a few sentences in the second half. His words showed his complicated feelings on the topic. He also asked Ji Yuan whether he was going to Wan Province, and if he was, when he might be leaving.

The second letter had been written almost two months ago. It was probably written to a friend in the county when he had learned that Ji Yuan was “off on a long journey,” writing to inform Ji Yuan that he had gone to Wan Province to study with three good friends of him from the academy, and that he hoped Mr. Ji would come too.

Yin Zhaoxian’s letter directly invited his good friend Ji Yuan to Wan Province, and invited him to attend either the Full-Month Banquet, or the Hundred Days Banquet.1 If Ji Yuan came earlier, they would hold a Full-Month Banquet; if he came later, they would hold a Hundred Days Banquet. His desire for Ji Yuan to come visit could not be more obvious.

“Hoooh… Master Yin won’t break off his friendship with me if I don’t go, will he?”

Yin Zhaoxian was a man of great integrity, and his wife, who lived beside him day and night, would not have any unstable energies that might harm the fetus. The baby would definitely be born at full term after ten months of pregnancy.2 Ji Yuan used his fingers to calculate a divination, and found that there was about a month left.

Since that was the case, Ji Yuan didn’t waste any more time. He went into his house to pack his things. He didn’t have much to bring with him, aside from his books, brushes, ink, paper, and two other sets of clothes.

His efforts these past few years had not been in vain. At the very least, he had figured out some clues about the art of Dimensional Storage in his sleeves. Although he didn’t know when he would be able to achieve the envisioned level of “storing items and weaving concealment around them, displaying their thousands of changes, devouring all things and returning them to their original state,” he had, at the very least, understood some of the principles of “change.” It referred to a change of body, as well as a change of location — it was very different from the ordinary art of Dimensional Storage, that simply stored things away by weaving an illusion around them.

Naturally, as his spiritual practice progressed, the amount of things that he could accommodate would increase. For now, he could still squeeze in some space to accommodate his current possessions.

Afterwards, he didn’t drop the paper crane off at home, but left the small pavilion directly. This time, he simply didn’t tell Hu Yun and Lu Shanjun about his departure. In any case, the two of them were cultivating fairly peacefully in the mountains.

. . .

According to “Reflections on the Governance of the Hundred Prefectures,” Lishun Prefecture in Wan Province had beautiful scenery and rich commerce. The Wan Province silk weaving industry in particular was reputed to be the best in the world. At the time when the book was written, it was said to be a place with stable governance and a harmonious people.

However, in today’s era, if this passage had been recounted by a storyteller, they would have added the words “once upon a time,” as the opening stage of Wan Province’s past.

The huge profits of the silk industry in Wan Province had greatly boosted its economy, but over time had also brought huge problems. These could be summed up in two words: greed and profit.

Wealthy merchants colluded with some officials to annex land and plant mulberry trees for profit.3 However, this did not bring any benefits to the farmers who lost their land, leading to complaints from the people in many parts of Wan Province. Resentment gradually accumulated, leading to a troubled period of time, and giving rise to evil spirits. This was one of the origins and proofs of the saying, “evil spirits work in troubled times.”

On this day, four scholars walked along the mountain road with book packs on their backs. They wore scarves over their heads, and their blue robes were stained with a little mud. These were the four students from Huiyuan Academy: Yin Qing, Lin Xinjie, Lei Yusheng, and Mo Xiu.

To be able to attend Lord Yin of Ji Province’s newborn banquet was a very prestigious opportunity in all respects, so Yin Qing just had just given them a polite invitation when the other three immediately agreed.

Naturally, it was impossible for the headmaster and the teachers of the academy to refuse this kind of petition for a study trip, so the group of friends embarked on the journey together.

“Oh, this path is really difficult to walk down. Mo Xiu, you really lived up to your name when you chose this road!”4

Lin Xinjie complained for the umpteenth time.

The man he had called out was so embarrassed that he couldn’t help but retort.

“When I suggested climbing Datong Mountain, weren’t you the one who agreed the most enthusiastically? What was it you said? Right, you wanted to enjoy the spring flowers in the forest and go hiking in the mountains. And now it’s all my fault?”

“Hey, alright, alright, everyone agreed at the time, we all just wanted to be greedy and take shortcuts.”

Lin Xinjie immediately changed the direction of his attack.

“Yusheng, you still have the guts to say that? If you hadn’t insisted on asking the driver to change our route and visit the inkstone workshop, we wouldn’t have taken such a long detour and wasted so much time, and we wouldn’t have been forced to come through here!”

“That’s right. It wasn’t me that suggested taking this route; if we didn’t take this route, it would have taken us more than a month to get to Lishun Prefecture!”

“You…”

Lei Yusheng was rendered speechless at the two men’s accusations. Yin Qing hurried to ease out the atmosphere.

“Stop talking, don’t say any more. This is not what any of us wanted. Who could have known that a section of the mountain road on Datong Mountain would collapse, making it impossible for carriages to pass? In any other case, it would have been wonderful to see the inkstone workshop and enjoy the mountains and flowers!”

“That’s right, that’s right!”

Lei Yusheng quickly agreed.

“Ah… This was a natural disaster, no one could have predicted it.”

“Yeah, it’s not like it’s a war crime or anything…”

Yin Qing shook his head and smiled.

“Let’s go, let’s go, stop sighing. If you talk too much, you’ll lose the energy to keep going. We’ve prepared plenty, we just need to hold on until we get out of Datong Mountain, and we’ll be fine. It’ll only take about seven or eight days to walk along the mountain path.”

Although Datong Mountain was barren, there was still a narrow mountain path. This was once the golden road of commerce at the start of Wan Province’s weaving industry. However, nowadays, silk and other goods were mostly transported by water or by other roads for more convenient mass shipment. This once-golden road gradually became deserted, and fewer and fewer people traveled along it.

But the road was still there, and they wouldn’t get lost following it. There were also some deserted outposts and waystations along the side of Datong Mountain along the way. Although most were not inhabited, they could still provide travelers with a place to rest.

The four of them walked, they finally saw a deserted waystation, half embedded in the hillside in front of them. There was a wisp of smoke coming out from it, as if someone was lighting a fire.

“Hurry up, let’s walk! I’m so tired, we’ll sleep there tonight!”

“Let’s go, let’s go, it seems that there are people there, let’s see if we can ask for a bowl of hot water!”

“Yeah!”

The four of them including Yin Qing were all energized at the sight and walked forward quickly. They soon seemed to hear footsteps and voices. Two men with axes slung behind their backs walked out of the gate of the deserted outpost. They glanced at the newcomers vigilantly. When they saw that it was just four scholars, they relaxed a bit and went back inside the deserted outpost.

Although there were still some tables and chairs in the deserted waystation, it was still relatively empty. The room was about five square-zhang, roughly equivalent to 200 square meters in Ji Yuan’s past life. There were more than a dozen people inside. In one corner were some large packs covered in straw raincoats and hats. Judging from their attire, these people were likely traveling merchants.

“Uncle Lu, there are four scholars outside. They are wearing long coats and carrying book packs. They are red-lipped and white-toothed, they should genuinely be good scholars.”5

The two people who had just returned inside reported.

“Alright, take a seat.”

It didn’t take long for Yin Qing and the other three to enter the deserted waystation. They walked in to see that there were more than a dozen people inside, and that almost everyone had a hatchet at hand. The merchants greeted the four of them, and the latter immediately felt nervous.

Yin Qing glanced at his friends, and had to step forward himself.

“We are scholars who have come on a study trip. The mountain road is difficult to travel. We would like to take shelter here and rest. Could we please ask to share some conveniences?”

An elderly man with gray hair looked Yin Qing over, and then said.

“This waystation is spacious. Our dozen or so traveling merchants won’t take up much space. Please make yourselves at home, sirs!”

“Thank you!”

Yin Qing bowed with cupped hands, and the three people beside him hurriedly followed.

“Thank you!”

“Thank you very much!”

“Boom…”

Suddenly, there was a loud crack of thunder outside, which frightened the two scholars on the edge of the group so much that they cried out, making some of the merchants laugh.

“It’s going to rain…”

The elder merchant took the lead and spoke.

The Hundred Days Banquet is similar to the Full Month Banquet, except instead of holding a feast to celebrate the baby’s 1 month survival, it’s 100 days survival.

“But Mango,” you might say, “pregnancies are 9 months!” Yes, you are correct. This is a traditional Chinese way of referring to full-term pregnancy. It’s equivalent to the Western 9 months.

Mulberry trees are fast-growing trees that are used to cultivate silkworms.

莫 (mò): do not, 休 (xiū): rest –> LOL his name literally means “do not rest,” so they’re making a pun. The literal translation of the sentence was something like “Mo Xiu, you really made sure we Mo Xiu when you chose this road”

Note that a lot of character judgements are tied to people’s appearance — this is one example. You can think of it like a confirmation that they are wealthy enough to not have to work in the fields, hence the white non-tanned skin and lack of chapped lips, and get enough vitamins to have their lips not be discolored. You could also take it as confirmation that they’re not ghosts

Chapter 217: A Letter From Long Ago

“Boom…”

A second clap of thunder sounded, much weaker, and echoed in the mountains.

Lin Xinjie and Lei Yusheng straightened their clothes, feeling slightly awkward. Their panic-stricken look just now was indeed a bit embarrassing.
