文曲星 (Wénqǔxīng): In Chinese mythology, Wenquxing is the god responsible for scholarly/literary pursuits and examinations, especially creative literature (i.e. not fame and fortune or memorization). Incredible scholars and imperial officials are associated with the incarnation of Wenquxing

Chapter 223: No Longer a Novice Official

[T/n: note on chapter title]1

As a household name in the literary circles of Dazhen, Yin Zhaoxian had now reached a point where he needed to pay attention to his image. After he had taken office in Wan Province and gotten to know the post for a while, he realized that he could not causally express certain emotions at will.

Even in front of his wife, Yin Zhaoxian still could not show the depression in his heart too clearly, for fear that his wife would be too worried. However, in front of Ji Yuan now, he had no such concerns. This was a rare opportunity for him to vent his emotions.

“Haah… I apologize for embarrassing myself in front of you, Mr. Ji!”

Yin Zhaoxian seemed a little agitated at this moment. It seemed that he had suffered quite a bit in his official position. Even someone with such a calm, even disposition had been brought to this point. Of course, it might also have been that he had been accumulating too much anger over a long period of time, so he grew quite agitated as he vented.

Ji Yuan held the teacup in his hand, but didn’t drink. He just blew gently onto the tea. The puff of breath also stirred the air in the room, dispersing some of the stagnant Qi and depression in his friend’s body, making him calm down significantly.

“Master Yin, it seems that the wealthy and prosperous region of Wan Province is not as outstanding as the other regions might imagine it to be?”

Hearing Mr. Ji finally addressing him as “Master Yin” again, Yin Zhaoxian felt inexplicably better for some reason, and then sighed.

“Ah… Mr. Ji, you aren’t aware that although Wan Province is indeed a prosperous region, some people are rich in gold and jade, while others don’t even have enough to eat. The former are few in number, while the latter are numerous. It is simply sick! Can you imagine, just a small community leader2…”

As Yin Zhaoxian spoke, he stretched out his right hand and pinched the tip of his little finger, almost shaking his hand as he gestured towards Ji Yuan.

“Such a trivial3 official, of absolutely no importance, actually owns over fifty qing4 of mulberry fields. How did he get these fields? How could he have acquired so much land while being a community leader his whole life?”

Yin Zhaoxian took a deep breath, picked up the teacup to take a sip, then added.

“What’s even more infuriating is that among these 50 qing of land, there are at least 1,000 mu5 of permanent farmland,6 which is the lifeblood of the people!”

Ji Yuan frowned. Now that he was thinking about it, the land of Wan Province that he had passed by when he was riding on the clouds did indeed have more mulberry forests and less grain. It seemed that it wasn’t that everyone was growing mulberry trees and raising silkworms in the hopes of getting rich, but rather that the people’s fields had all been annexed?

Perhaps in the beginning, farmers were indeed tempted by the profits of planting mulberry trees and raising silkworms. Then, after suffering various losses due to natural or man-made disasters, the farmers bought back their mulberry fields at a seemingly “fair” price. Now, there were more mulberry trees than fields, and this industry was strictly controlled by the rich and powerful, leaving the common people with nowhere to go.

With this small picture from the events in Lishun Prefecture, the overarching trend the entire Wan Province was that many farmers and common people had very little land that they actually owned. If they wanted to have enough food, most of the time they had no choice but to rely on the people who owned their land, but growing mulberry trees and raising silkworms did not require that many people. Most of the profits the farmers made went to wealthy families. In addition, they had to bear the same taxes by themselves, so their lives were quite difficult.

And, more importantly, the only reward the farmers who helped the wealthy families tend to the silkworms and make silk received was money. If Wan Province did not have enough grain, they would have to buy it from other places. Buying grain allowed for the profit to be exploited by many merchants. Price fluctuations were not determined by the farmers. And who had the final say on the fluctuations in grain prices? They would naturally be affected by natural disasters, but it was also the wealthy merchants and officials from these same wealthy families who had the final say.

What could you do when you didn’t have enough money? Mortgage your land for the coming year!

After listening to Yin Zhaoxian’s analysis, even Ji Yuan, an “immortal” in the eyes of ordinary people, could not help but feel a chill down his spine. Over the years, the land of the farmers in Wan Province, upon which the latter depended for their survival, had been eaten away bit by bit, just like how the silkworms in Wan Province ate away at the mulberry leaves…

Yin Zhaoxian drank some tea from his teacup, then sneered again.

“Everyone in Dazhen says: Bing Province’s grain, Wan Province’s silk — hah, how can the situation of the people of Wan Province be compared with that of Bing Province?”

Ji Yuan couldn’t help but nod. He had stayed in Bing Province for quite some time. Although he had been cultivating on Yun Mountain for most of that period, he had heard the abundance of laughter from the busy farmers during the rice harvesting season in Bing Province.

“So, Master Yin, are you taking action there?”

Yin Zhaoxian shook his head.

“If I could strike at everyone I see, why would I feel so depressed? In the past two years since I came to Wan Province, I first carefully observed the people’s sentiments and did not show any indications of favor to either side. As I got to know Lishun Prefecture and Wan Province more and more, I also increasingly understood that this was a matter where pulling one hair could move the whole body.7 Though it may seem like just a hair to me, I cannot act so easily!”

Although Ji Yuan didn’t understand the constraints of officialdom, his friend’s words were enough to show that Master Yin had a thorough understanding of his position, and was by no means a fool in this web. JI Yuan put aside some of his concerns.

“Master Yin, you hold the position of prefect, and you have connections in the imperial court. Why are you so wary?”

As Ji Yuan spoke, he picked up the teapot and refilled his friend’s teacup, just like those afternoons where they chatted with each other in Ju’an Pavilion.

Yin Zhaoxian had calmed down now. Just like Ji Yuan, he did not have any reservations, so he simply picked up his teacup and drank it.

“Although the imperial court’s inspectors have visited Wan Province and reported that it has been fine over the years, this alone is not enough. I am afraid that there are also deep-rooted interests at play in the imperial court. Lishun Prefecture and the entire Wan Province are of great concern to many people!”

Yin Zhaoxian’s speech at this moment was completely like that of a veteran official who had been in his position for a long time, rather than that of a rookie official who had just been promoted from his top scholar position a few years ago.

Although Ji Yuan felt bad for the people of Wan Province, after this simple conversation, he was no longer too worried about his friend, so he jokingly asked a question — though there was no teasing tone in his voice.

“And then Master Yin was sent to Wan Province. Wouldn’t those interested groups regard you as a thorn in their side?”

“I’m not enough to be a thorn in their side, but a certain wariness is inevitable. Particularly when I first took office, I was on guard day and night. Haha, thinking back on it now, it was ridiculous!”

Yin Zhaoxian changed the subject at this point.

“However, even though I had come here to observe people’s sentiments carefully, I did not make any unnecessary movements. I appeared to be minding my own business, which made them relax. Now, the officials of Lishun Prefecture and even Wan Province all think that the scholar who won all three top places in the imperial examinations is just very talented, and was sent to Wan Province to gild his reputation; sooner or later, he will be promoted to the capital. They don’t want anything to happen during my tenure, so it has all been stable!”

“It’s ironic: after that, more and more people came to me to give gifts, and their positions have risen higher and higher, from small households to wealthy families.”

“Oh? Are they all valuable gifts?”

Ji Yuan reached out and touched the teapot, making the tea return to the right temperature. He refilled a cup for himself and another for his friend.

“They’re all very valuable — or, rather, most of it is money! White silver, and shining gold. After all, everyone is optimistic about this Yin Zhaoxian’s future promotion. Who knows, I might become their new backer in the court in the future.”

Yin Zhaoxian smirked, and then said something to Ji Yuan in a joking manner.

“I will not refuse anyone who comes. I accept them all!”

When Ji Yuan heard these words, although he didn’t show it on his face, inside he was stunned. However, he realized something immediately: he still had confidence in his friend’s character. This confidence came both from his understanding of Master Yin, and also from the pure and righteous condensed aura surrounding Master Yin’s body at this moment.

“Why, does Master Yin plan to use courtesy first, and then force to settle accounts at the right time?”8

“My parents raised me honorably,9 and Ji Yuan understands me well! Every time, every gift, every bribe, I remember it all clearly!”

It was a rare occasion for Yin Zhaoxian to act mysterious in front of his good friend.

“If we were to identify who among the entire Dazhen court, government, and public hates corruption the most — aside from me, Yin Zhaoxian, it would have to be the current emperor.”

Ji Yuan looked at Yin Zhaoxian, shook his head, and sighed.

“As far as I knows, the old emperor is obsessed with that auspicious omen and is now preparing for a Water and Land Assembly,10 calling for all the ‘distinguished talents’11 from all walks of life in Dazhen to convene. It’s hard to say how much of his attention he will afford to Wan Province.”

Apart from a frown, Yin Zhaoxian didn’t show any special expression. He would not say that his friend’s tone was disrespectful, exactly.

Ji Yuan smiled and continued to speak leisurely.

“He is key to the fate of Dazhen, respected by thousands. He can prolong his life, but if he wishes to seek immortality, he must give up his position. It is impossible for him to do both.”

Yin Zhaoxian did not dwell on this issue.

“His Majesty might no longer be young and strong, but he will not remain indifferent to the affairs of Wan Province. Moreover, the ambitions Prince Jin is also involved in this matter. Half a year ago, I had secretly reported to my findings to His Majesty through His Royal Highness Prince Jin. I didn’t say much, just informing him of the bribes the gift givers gave me. I’m sure Mr. Ji can guess how much the total was.”

Seeing Master Yin’s expression, Ji Yuan figured that he might as well guess a high number, so he spoke.

“Fifty thousand taels of silver?”

Yin Zhaoxian shook his head.

“No, no!”

“One hundred thousand taels?”

Seeing that Master Yin was still shaking his head, Ji Yuan immediately continued.

“Fifty thousand taels of gold?”

“Mr. Ji, guess again. Some of the metals I obtained shone yellow, while others shone white;12 even more were rare or antiques.”

Ji Yuan grinned. He calculated a divination with his right hand hidden in his sleeve, and then spoke with a hint of surprise.

“It was actually 210,000 taels of gold!?”13

Yin Zhao was taken aback by the precise data, and then nodded in answer.

“Yes, after a few years, if you include all the treasures and artifacts, it should be worth about two million taels of silver. Haha, it’s almost equal to the annual tax that Wan Province pays the national treasury every half year!”

“I am the prefect of Lishun Prefecture. Although I do indeed have some favorable prospects, I have received a disproportionate amount of benefits during my two-year term. How much money was taken from the people and from the imperial court? How could the emperor not be furious?”

Yin Zhaoxian sneered when he spoke these words.

“I wrote it all in the secret letter I sent through Prince Jin. After receiving my secret report, His Majesty smashed his favorite set of teacups in the Imperial Study!”

He had become foolishly complacent in his old age. Although many emperors in past dynasties had been similar, Emperor Yuan De did not think he would be one of them. Now that he found himself being deceived by those serving under him, one could only imagine the anger in his heart.

Hearing this, Ji Yuan knew that his friend already had a plan in mind. He felt half relieved and half emotional.

‘Master Yin is no longer a novice official!’

The word “Novice” is 稚子 (zhìzǐ) lit. “young child,” meant to signify innocence, naïveté, and inexperience

里 (lǐ): ancient administrative unit of 25 families (also: neighborhood/borough), 正 (zhèng): main/principal/etc.

芝麻绿豆 (zhīmalǜdòu) Lit. “sesame mungbean” but together makes the word “trivial,” in the sense of “as tiny and stupid as beans” which I find hilarious

1 qing = 0.066667 km2, so 50 qing = 3.3333 km2, about the size of Central Park in NYC, over 2.5 times the size of Monaco.

Permanent farmland is land used for crops that don’t need to be replanted every year (orchards, vineyards, plantations). Contrasted with arable land, which is for annually harvested crops.

i.e. changing one thing can have drastic effects.

Chapter 224: The Dragon Fell At Guangdong Lake

Yin Zhaoxian was quite emotional at these words. When he had been appointed to Lishun Prefecture in Wan Province, and was about to return to Ning’an County in Ji Province before officially taking up his post, Prince Jin’s Royal Tutor, Li Mushu, had disguised himself and mysteriously visited him at the waystation.

At that time, the purpose of Li Mushu’s visit was not made clear. He merely wished to tell him that although his appointment to Wan Province was a gesture of support from the Emperor, many people were nervous. He asked Yin Zhaoxian to remain calm and stick to his heart when he went to Wan Province.

In any case, Li Mushu hadn’t explained the exact meaning behind his words at that time, but Yin Zhaoxian could still make out some of the unspoken meaning. It seemed that the tutor was worried about safety and whether he would be changed by his trip to Wan province; that kind of concern could not be faked.

So, on the eve of him taking office, Prince Jin sent a letter to Ning’an County, bluntly stating that he had secretly sent people to replace the originally assigned attendants and other staff. Yin Zhaoxian did not shirk on his responsibilities, so to this day there were still many skilled martial artists by his side.

However, regarding his governmental affairs, Yin Zhaoxian was simply complaining to Ji Yuan, and had no intention of going into detail. This was not the reason he had invited his good friend over.

“Okay, okay, I couldn’t help but start complaining when I saw Mr. Ji. Please, I hope you don’t blame me. This time, I’d like to invite you to attend our child’s banquet, but you might have to wait a while.”

Yin Zhaoxian was of course very happy that Ji Yuan had come to visit, but the child had not been born yet, so it was of course impossible to hold a celebration immediately.

“Well, when is the Madam due?”

Ji Yuan asked the question knowingly. Yin Zhaoxian answered with a cheerful smile.

“The doctor said that my wife is nurturing the fetus well, and the fetal Qi is stable. She will be full-term in about half a month.”

“Oh, not bad, not bad. I must congratulate Master Yin in advance!”

“Hahahaha…Thank you very much. I have already arranged Mr. Ji’s accommodation, you will be staying in the guest house at the back of our residence.”

Ji Yuan also smiled and bowed with cupped hands. There as no need to stand on ceremony with close friends.

The two men almost lost track of time as they chatted together. Although Yin Zhaoxian was just complaining about his suffering, half a day had passed before they knew it. After treating him to a home-cooked meal, he wanted to accompany Ji Yuan to the guest house in person, but he had official business to attend to, so he had his servants do it for him.

Ji Yuan was led by a servant, walking through the small back garden, bypassing a corridor, and arrived at the guest quarters of the mansion.

“Mr. Ji, please come in. It’s right ahead.”

The servant politely led Ji Yuan forward. After they had arrived at the guesthouse, he opened the door to show Ji Yuan the furnishings inside the house, then bid him farewell.

“Mr. Ji, please rest here. If you have any requests, just let me know. You can call me from the corridor. I will take my leave now!”

“Okay, thank you for your help!”

Ji Yuan bowed to express his thanks, and the servant hurriedly returned the salute.

“Hey…”

When the servant had walked quite a distance, he turned back to look at Ji Yuan, only to see that the latter had not gone into the house. He just stood at the door, gazing at the courtyard and the sky. He seemed neither arrogant nor restrained, and his demeanor was extraordinarily graceful.1

Some servants in the mansion were actually a little curious about Ji Yuan’s arrival. Their master had never hosted anyone in his home, and never let anyone stay overnight. Those who came from far away to give gifts had all stayed in waystations and inns. But this Mr. Ji was so special; he ate and lived in the mansion. It seemed that he was indeed the master’s close friend.

Ji Yuan casually looked around his room. Apart from the basic bed, table, desk, and chair, there were also four treasures of the study2 and a chess set. It was not luxurious, but it was comprehensive.

After looking around, Ji Yuan did not rest here, but instead took a step out the door. Phantom mist and smoke appeared under his feet, and he went straight out of the mansion.

The streets of Lishun Prefecture were still prosperous. Merchants from all over Dazhen and even the neighboring countries came here to buy silk fabrics. The whole city relied on the silk industry, and it was currently visibly prospering. There were inns, restaurants, and tea houses everywhere. There were even more brothels and gambling houses than in other cities.

This was just Lishun Prefecture; the provincial capital, Yunbo Prefecture, was probably even a step up.

Ji Yuan wandered through the streets and alleyways. By relying on a combination of keen hearing and his blurred vision, he was able to walk without hindrance, and listen to people’s discussions. By following certain voices and occasionally asking for directions, he made his way to the Temple Administration Lane.

From the time he first came to this world, Ji Yuan already knew that, although local gods such as City Gods protected a region, they could not take initiative to interfere with worldly affairs, particularly when it came to situations involving the general fate of humanity. The more one relied on the power of a certain place, the more true this restriction became.

This kind of fear arose from the impact the changes in humanity might have on the gods themselves, but also from the impact on their cultivation and state of mind. There were quite a few anecdotal lessons from history to learn from in this respect.

Gradually, it became an unwritten law that the Yin world of the underworld could not interfere in the affairs of the Yang world of the living, particularly with regards to some major events.

Even so, whenever a dynasty collapsed, some local gods like the city gods would also be destroyed, because they were too deeply connected to the fate of the dynasty. Therefore, the underworlds of various regions were secretive about such matters, and felt very conflicted.

Of course, Ji Yuan had gone to the Temple Administration Lane to visit the City God Temple. Although the city god himself could not interfere, it was impossible for the underworld to be unaware of the current happenings. He did not go there to investigage, but rather to see how Lishun Prefecture was dealing with the rise of evil spirits and resentment energy within its jurisdiction.

No matter which city it was, Temple Administration Lane was always one of the most bustling and prosperous locations in the city; Lishun Prefecture was no different.

As Ji Yuan passed through the streets around Temple Administration Lane, the sounds of hawking and haggling grew much more lively. There were tourists from all over. After all, the area near the City God Temple was a must-visit place for sightseeing, and it was also where the most delicious restaurants and food stands gathered.

“Hey, sir, come buy a few sticks of incense. You can dedicate them to the Lord City God, it will ensure you pass the imperial examination or make a lot of money!”

“Ah, sir, this incense is excellent. It’s a newly formed work of sandalwood, five coins a stick. I also have some excellent red candles!”3

Ji Yuan walked over and saw that several stall owners were selling sandalwood candles.

“No, I’m not here to burn incense.” … “No need, I don’t need any, really, I don’t need any…”

Some booths had two or three people minding them; the stall owners were so enthusiastic that they even walked out from behind the stalls to introduce their wares and sell incense and candles to passers-by. Ji Yuan refused repeatedly.

Moreover, this incense was too expensive. One stick of incense was equivalent to the price of an entire meal of noodles in other places. Plus, he was afraid that it wasn’t just the incense that was expensive.4

The entire City God Temple was crowded with tourists. There were almost no quiet places in any of the halls. Ji Yuan followed a group of pilgrims through the temple halls and corridors, passing by numerous well-dressed people praying to the gods.

Most of the things they sought were money and material benefits; there were very few ordinary people who came to worship the local gods.

An older temple steward stood next to Ji Yuan. Seeing that the latter was just walking around and not burning incense, he figured that the man was likely a tourist. Although his clothes were not extravagant, he had an extraordinary demeanor. The black jade hairpin on his head was crystal clear and shining under the light, obviously a priceless item.

Seeing that Ji Yuan had stopped walking and was standing to the side for a moment, the steward walked up to him and tentatively asked him a question.

“Would the gentleman like to burn some incense for the City God? If you donate some money, the City God will bless you, and everything will go smoothly.”

Ji Yuan glanced back at the old man, and then at the statue of the City God.

“Haha… Your Lord City God is quite popular. Unfortunately, he wouldn’t dare accept so much more incense.”

“Uh… what do you mean, Sir?”

Ji Yuan shook his head.

“There’s no deeper meaning, just take it literally.”

The City God did not dare accept the incense power and wishes from the people who gathered here to burn incense because most of them were motivated by profit and self-interest. This incense power was “toxic.” Even the divinations conducted in the temple would not respond to certain people — what they were doing was just a simple probability calculation.

“Sir, you are correct!”

A sigh and a voice came from the side. A middle-aged man with a sallow complexion, wearing a black robe and a black square cap, walked over and bowed to Ji Yuan with cupped hands.

Ji Yuan sincerely returned the greeting.

“How about we talk outside the temple?”

“It’s better to obey than be respectful!”

The man and Ji Yuan traded questions as they walked out of the main hall of the City God Temple one after another. The temple steward behind them scratched his head, feeling puzzled. It seemed he had encountered two strange people today.

The two did not walk through the main gate, but instead passed through a side gate where there were relatively few people, and walked leisurely to a small river right outside the City God Temple.

There were still people walking about and playing outside here, but it seemed more peaceful.

When they arrived outside, the man in the black robe bowed solemnly to Ji Yuan.

“Mr. Ji has ash-gray eyes that see all, and inky black jade hair. He is a man of deep inner wisdom, one who has returned to the natural origin. If what I have guessed is correct, you must be the legendary Mr. Ji, correct?”

Huh?

Ji Yuan was confused. The legendary… Mr. Ji?

‘My reputation has spread so far, it has even made its way to Wan Province. What does he mean by legendary?’

If he had mentioned his reputation in Ji Province, Ji Yuan would have thought it reliable, but this was Wan Province.

Ji Yuan could only smile politely while returning the bow.

“I don’t dare claim to be a legend, but my surname is Ji and my name is Yuan. I am the ‘Mr. Ji’ who meets the characteristics that City God Li described; it seems that is indeed me.”

“Sure enough, it was Mr. Ji. I was just making a tentative guess. I never expected to meet you in person.”

City God Li smiled.

“Dazhen is now facing a troubled time. It is a great blessing for our Dazhen to have Mr. Ji here to quell the turmoil everywhere.”

‘Wait, what are you talking about? ’

Ji Yuan was a little confused by the words of this City God Li. Could it be that the world had changed so much after he had slept for more than half a year?

“I wonder if the Lord City God might explain it clearly. I am unfortunately at a loss, sir!”

“I dare not receive the title Lord or Sir in front of Mr. Ji. Please don’t embarrass me…”

The City God gave a wry smile.

“We, the people of Dazhen, know that the fate of humanity is uncertain. The hearts of ones such as we city gods tremble like a candle flame in the wind. Before this…”

“ROOOOOOOAR!”

A dragon’s roar sounded in the distant sky. Ji Yuan and the City God’s expressions immediately changed, and they looked to the northern sky.

“ROOOO…ROOOO…ROOOOOAR…”

“Boom…”

The onslaught of roars from a dragon that was clearly in an abnormal state resounded across the sky. Many people in the city looked up to see a circle of rain clouds on the northern horizon. Strange sounds, like the roars of some giant beast, or the wailing of an old cow, echoed across the sky, accompanied by lightning and thunder.

Many people looked on from afar and noticed a cloud on the northern horizon that seemed as though it were falling from the sky. It looked more like a dragon than some giant snake.

“Boom…”

Amidst the thunder and lightning, dark clouds quickly covered the sky, and the daylight darkened.

A dragon’s cry and dragon’s call were actually two different things. The dragon’s call was long, distant, and majestic, while the dragon’s cry sounded like an old cow, mixed with chaos.5 It was obvious that this dragon was in a very bad state.

Ji Yuan’s first reaction was to worry about whether it was a Ying family member, but the sound of this dragon’s roar was obviously not familiar.

“Where is it?”

“To answer Mr. Ji, it should be in the direction of Guangdong Lake. It is less than a hundred li away from here, and also within the boundaries of Lishun Prefecture.”

The City God answered Ji Yuan’s question with a serious expression.

Chapter 225: Water Energy Dispersing Wildly

The people in the city were a little panicked when they heard the roar from the distant horizon just now, and then saw the dark clouds rolling in, lightning and thunder flashing in an instant. The crowd in front of the City God Temple was also discussing it.

“What’s going on? The sky was so clear just now…”

“Hey, did you hear that sound in the distance just now? The one that wasn’t thunder!”

“I heard it, it sounded so creepy!”

“It was like the howling of an old bull, so scary!”

“Yeah, I got goosebumps!”

“Did you see that cloud just now?”

“Yeah, it looked like something fell from the sky.”

“Could it be a dragon?”

“Hey, what is that nonsense?”

“Boom…”

Amidst the chatter, lightning and thunder suddenly exploded overhead, startling everyone.

“It’s going to rain, let’s go back quickly.”

“Yes, yes!”

“Let’s go, I’m going home too.”

……

The people in the city visibly quickened their pace. Some simply hid in teahouses and restaurants because the weather was changing too quickly.

“Wooooo… whooooooosh…”

Ji Yuan and the City God of Lishun Prefecture had merely been staring into the distance for a short while, but there were already gusts of dark wind whipping around them, and the tourists that had originally been playing by the river outside the temple had all run away.

Ji Yuan looked down at the creek not too far away and saw a paper kite lying beside the willow tree. It had obviously been dropped here by someone who had left in a hurry, and was now tossing and turning in the gusts of northern wind.

The City God of Lishun Prefecture kept looking at the sky, staring in the direction of Guangdong Lake to the north. It had been some time since he had stopped hearing the dragon’s roars.

“There is a vast expanse of water energy dispersing wildly, causing storm clouds and thunder to gather. Mr. Ji, shall we go to Guangdong Lake to investigate?”

City God Li, who had been silent for a while in apprehension, was the first to speak, making this suggestion to Ji Yuan.

“That’s exactly what I was thinking!”

Ji Yuan just happened to return his gaze to the horizon at this moment. With his supernatural eyes wide open, he could see the water energy in the distance rising up like a dam that had just been opened.

The reason why the falling silhouette of the dragon appeared more like a cloud was because of the water energy wildly leaking, causing the haze to wrap around the dragon’s body. Similarly, this was also the reason for the quick pace at which the dark clouds were spreading.

Although Ji Yuan was also very curious about what the City God of Lishun Prefecture had commented just previously, he had his priorities in order; the most urgent task was naturally to go to Guangdong Lake.

“Let’s go!”

Ji Yuan leapt into the sky, riding on a nearby gale. City God Li in a black robe turned into a ray of rainbow light and flew beside Ji Yuan.

Even at this time, they did not travel as fast as possible, the man and the ghost flew through the air while using their energy to observe carefully. They constantly watched the direction in the sky where the dragon’s silhouette fell.

At the very beginning, it seemed to have been descending from the sky, but later it finally lost its stability, and the dragon fell from the heavens with a roar.

“Boom…”

The dark clouds in the sky were sinking lower and lower, and lightning flashed among the clouds. Ji Yuan and the City God seemed to be flying with the dark clouds right above their heads, as if they could be struck by thunder at any time.

Guangdong Lake had naturally been mentioned in the book “Reflections on the Governance of the Hundred Prefectures” that Ji Yuan had read. It was one of the four major lakes in Dazhen. The vast Guangdong Lake connected to many large rivers, and there was no exact record of the depths of its waters. It was also known as the Hundred-Li Guangdong Lake.1

In fact, Guangdong Lake did not only belong to Lishun Prefecture, but shared a border with three prefectures. However, Lishun Prefecture claimed the largest area of the lake, so although the people in the bordering areas would still be under the jurisdiction of their respective prefectures, Guangdong Lake nominally belonged to Lishun Prefecture.

There were no major counties on the other side of the lake. Once it had been confirmed that the incident had been caused by some kind of monster or spirit, the City God of Lishun Prefecture was duty-bound to investigate. It was not appropriate to send over a daytime patrol group in this situation. With Mr. Ji, the great virtuous immortal, by his side, his heart felt a lot more at ease.

Ji Yuan’s brows furrowed as he rode the wind. Most of the dragons and flood dragons were not small-time monsters, particularly in Dazhen; they were well-known, powerful figures among the aquatic creatures. And all the various dragons in Dazhen were strictly well-behaved because of the moderating and restraining existence of the True Dragon Ying Hong. This matter was very serious. However, there was a City God coming to investigate it together with him; whether in terms of experience or confidence, Ji Yuan felt more at ease.

At the back of the government building of Lishun Prefecture, Yin Zhaoxian strode through the garden and down the corridors, hurriedly making his way to the guest house, followed by a servant of the guest wing and another servant of the main mansion.

His voice already rang out in the air before he had even arrived at the guest house.

“Mr. Ji… Mr. Ji… I have something to ask you! Mr. Ji…”

The three of them arrived outside the guest house one after another, but found that the door was closed.

“Knock knock knock…”

“Mr. Ji, I have something to ask please!”

“Knock knock knock…”

“Mr. Ji, are you resting? Yin Zhaoxian needs to speak with you!”

“Knock knock… creeeak…”

Yin Zhaoxian knocked on the door harder, but the door was directly knocked open. It turned out that it had not been latched from the inside.

He looked into the room. There was no clutter on the tables, chairs, or other surfaces. The bedding was neat and intact. There was no sign of Ji Yuan.

Yin Zhaoxian turned around and frowned at the servant in charge of the guest house.

“Didn’t you say that Mr. Ji was in the guest house? Why is he gone? When did he leave?”

The servant was also looking inside. Hearing Yin Zhaoxian’s words, he was very puzzled and scratched his head in confusion.

“Uh… Master, Mr. Ji was indeed here before. This servant was in the outer garden this whole time, and I didn’t see Mr. Ji go out…”

On Yin Zhaoxian’s other side, the servant in official uniform looked at the guest house attendant seriously.

“Did you doze off and fall asleep, or did you simply neglect your duties and lie to your superiors?”

“Oh, hey, this is the first time the master has let someone stay in his house for such a long period of time. Everyone is talking about how amazing Mr. Ji is. How could I dare neglect him? Besides, the people who cleaned the yard with me didn’t see Mr. Ji leave either, sir!”

Yin Zhaoxian looked at the rolling thunderclouds thoughtfully and waved his hand.

“Okay, let’s leave it at that. Let me know when Mr. Ji comes back.”

Yin Zhaoxian had actually come here at this time to ask Ji Yuan about what had just happened. His righteous aura made him much more sensitive to the sounds and the silhouette of the dragon in the sky than ordinary people. He also had prior experience with supernatural things, so the first thing he thought of was to ask his good friend about it.

But now, it seemed that Mr. Ji must have left to check the moment he heard the sound.

As for why the servants didn’t see him, how could they possibly notice when an immortal being wanted to leave?

“Boom… boom… boom…”

Lighting flashed and illuminated the earth.

“Crash, splash…shhh shhh…”

Rain began to pour in an instant. Within the span of a few breaths, the ground had gone from dry and dusty to frothing as if it were covered in white beads. This was the first time this kind of heavy rain had been seen since the beginning of spring this year.

About a quarter of an hour ago, in Shuanggongqiao Village, thirty miles south of Guangdong Lake, the villagers were still busy with their work.

“Boom…”

Suddenly, the sound of thunder exploded in the sky overhead.

“Oh my goodness!”

“That scared me…”

“Was there a sudden clap of thunder on a sunny day?”

“Hey, the sky is getting dark!”

“Look, look, the clouds in the sky…”

“Hooh…”

The clap of thunder frightened almost everyone in the village, both old and young. It was just too sudden. But when they looked up, they found that the storm clouds seemed to be growing out of the sky like magic…

“Roooar…”

“Boom…”

The dragon’s roar echoed far and wide. Although it mingled with the sound of thunder, it was unique and strange, causing many people to look at each other in surprise.

“ROOOO…ROOOO…ROOOOOAR…”

The dragon beast’s roars and painful cries continued to echo across the sky. The villagers were gradually so frightened that they got goosebumps.

“What are you standing there for? Go grab your things!”

“Yes, yes…”

Seeing that the rain clouds were getting bigger and lower, all the villagers from every household hurriedly put away their belongings that were hung up to dry outside. The large grain field at the entrance to the village was in chaos, with large bamboo sieves, silkworm beds, crops, clothes, and all other items in complete disarray, all needing to be put away.

“The sky! Look at the sky!”

Someone in the village shouted in a voice that sounded more like a scream. Then the people around them also began shouting, “look at the sky, look at the sky,” making many of the villagers raise their heads again.

At this very moment, there was a “long cloud” among the other clouds in the sky, as if it had torn through the cloud layer and was falling down from the heavens. As it fell, there seemed to be an endless trail of smoke-like vapor that trailed out of it continuously. This long “cloud shadow” fell from the sky, trailing a stream of mist along its path, and getting closer and closer to the ground.

“Hey, hey, why does that cloud look so much like a dragon?”

“It does look a bit like that…”

“Oh, it’s getting lower and lower!”

“It’s going to fall, it’s going to fall!”

“Run… something is about to fall!”

“Don’t worry about the things on the ground, just run–“

“Run!”

“Aaaaah…”

……

The villagers in the grain field at the entrance to the village fled in a panic, instinctively running towards their own homes.

“Whoooosh… whoooo… whoooo…”

As the “cloud shadow” fell, a strong wind tore through the air and launched water droplets at the ground. The faint cry of an old cow could vaguely be heard.

Many villagers subconsciously looked up to the sky while running. Then, at last… the dragon-shaped silhouette touched the ground.

“Boom…”

It was as if an earth dragon rolled over; the ground shook unceasingly. Many villagers who couldn’t run far enough were knocked to the ground by the violent earthquake. Many others felt their feet shaking, and could not stand steadily.

Then a stronger gust of wind swept in.

Whoo… whooo… whooo… Swish…

Many people who had originally remained standing were knocked off their feet by the shock wave of the airflow. The roofs of many of the houses near the grain fields were blown so intensely that they rattled as if they were about to be carried away.

In this crazy gale, most people could only squat down with their heads in their arms. Fortunately, it came and went quickly.

In the aftermath, the valley at the entrance of Shuanggongqiao Village seemed to have been engulfed in a dull fire. Billowing clouds of vapor burst out madly like thick smoke from a fire, rushing towards the sky and spreading throughout the village and its surroundings.

The sunlight above had been completely blocked. Endless dark clouds, accompanied by the rumbling thunder, quickly filled the sky above Lishun Prefecture.

The villagers of Shuanggongqiao Village seemed be surrounded by a deep fog in that moment. They could barely even see their hands in front of them. After stumbling around in a panic, they could only stare at the ground and move carefully towards the nearest houses. Only after reaching shelter could they breathe a sigh of relief.

“Boom… Boom, boom… Crack… Boom…”

The sound of thunder and the terrifying atmosphere at the moment made people in the village tremble with fear.

“Clatter, crash, shhhh…”

Amidst the terror and darkness, the heavy rain finally arrived; however, it made the villagers feel inexplicably relieved.

i.e. hundreds of li in depth

Chapter 226: Exorcise and Bind Demons

Many villagers, especially those out in the fields, did not have time to flee all the way back to their homes, and so took shelter in nearby houses. There were often more than seven or eight people in a room.

Although the dense fog gradually dissipated after the heavy rain began to fall, the visibility was still not very good due to the downpour. However, the villagers still all looked towards the big valley beyond the fields in the distance.

Something had fallen just now, and the entire Shuanggongqiao1 Village could hear and feel the way it shook the earth beneath them. Those who were close by in particular were still frightened.

“Rooo…roo…”2

The sounds that resembled an old cow wailing were faint, drowned out by the sound of the rain if one did not listen carefully. However, now that the villagers were focused, they could naturally hear it.

“Was someone’s cow hit?”3

“No way, there are no cows tied to up in the fields… But those big sieves and the vegetables must have all been smashed…”

“What is falling from the sky? I can’t see it clearly from here!”

“Yeah, there’s still a lot of heavy haze over there… But it looks like it was something big… Could it be a falling star?”

An old man who was also the head of one of the households stood at the door, looking towards the valley fields. He had a frown on his face, but remained silent. After the others had talked for a while, he suddenly spoke.

“I heard that the… the roar of a dragon… sometimes it sounds like an old cow…”

All the discussions around him stopped at once, and everyone turned to look at the old man.

“Uncle… Please don’t scare us…”

“Yeah, uncle… Are you trying to say that a dragon fell from the sky?”

“But this storm is really strange… Maybe what Uncle Zhao said is…”

“Hasn’t there been a legend passed down through generations about a dragon in Guangdong Lake…?”

“Hey… No way…”

All the people around them were beginning to tremble with fear.

“Rooo…”

Hearing the cries in the distance, everyone subconsciously shrank towards the inside of the house, no longer daring to stay at the door.

Although dragons were found throughout all kinds of myths, some of them were demonized. The legend of the divine dragon that brought rain was widely circulated, but there were also many myths of dragons sweeping away houses with their tails, tornadoes destroying villages and fields, and even some of dragons eating people.

Just like Lord Ye’s love of dragons,4 everyone spoke of dragons with relish, but at this moment all that came to mind were terrifying thoughts.

“Hey… Look at the fog over there, it’s a bit similar in shape…”

“Don’t…don’t scare me…”

“Oh, no, if its full body is in that fog, how big would it be? Wouldn’t it be enough for it to eat us all in this room in just a few bites?”

“Hey, hey, stop talking, it’s scary!”

“Boom…”

Another bright bolt of lightning flashed, illuminating the village as brightly as a sunny day. Many people saw the huge black shadow in the distance, and the village was silent for a while.

. . .

When Ji Yuan and the City God of Lishun Prefecture flew over Shuanggongqiao Village, the village was shrouded in a much heavier rain than the places around it. No villagers dared leave the houses they were hiding in.

Using his water avoidance technique, the surrounding water flowed over Ji Yuan and City God Li without touching their bodies. They could overlook the area below and around them from a height of several dozen zhang.

“Boom…”

Under the flash of lightning, the land around Shuanggongqiao Village was instantly illuminated. An enormous black dragon with a sinuous body lay at the entrance to the village. The large fields supported its head and nearly half of its body, and its back half and dragon tail swung outside the village. The dragon was about forty to fifty zhang long,5 with a body of varying thickness. Even as it was being drenched in the heavy rain, there was still a steady stream of mist seeping out of its body surface.

Ji Yuan looked at Guangdong Lake in the distance. The huge lake was only about 20 to 30 li away. Although the falling dragon had made such a loud noise, there was no response from Guangdong Lake.

“It seems that this dragon should be that Lord Mo from Guangdong Lake.”

City God Li frowned as he spoke to Mr. Ji beside him, but he couldn’t see any reaction on the other’s face.

Ji Yuan stared at the dragon below them with his deep eyes. As his supernatural eyes opened, he could see that the black dragon was seriously leaking spiritual energy and water essence; it overall physical condition was extremely bad.

There was a shard of ice crystal in the middle of his spine, which had obviously been created by water vapor freezing to cover over a wound.

‘It was caused by a fight! ’

This was the common thought in ​Ji Yuan and City God Li’s minds.

Ji Yuan didn’t say anything. After hesitating for a moment, he took the lead descending down from the sky, and City God Li followed immediately after.

The man and the god descended at a constant speed. Their movements were very gentle, trying not to stimulate the black dragon at this moment. Even monsters like this could sometimes act like wild beasts, and it would be even more dangerous if they were critically injured.

“Splash…”

Ji Yuan stepped on the ground with both feet, causing a little water to splash, while the City God touched down silently.

“Rooo…haah…”

The black dragon tiredly cracked his eyes open and saw the City God in a black robe, the divine light on his body having not been deliberately concealed. He couldn’t see the other person next to him, but it was obviously not a mortal.

“I am Li Baotian, the City God of Lishun Prefecture. Today, I suddenly saw a dragon falling from a distance. When I came here to look into it, I realized that it was Lord Mo of Guangdong Lake. My Lord, why are you here?”

“Rooo…”

The enormous black dragon let out a wail, but it just sounded like an old cow’s roar, without any discernible speech.

“Crash…”

The rain continued to wash over the dragon’s body. It struggled to support itself on its four claws, and its body slowly left the ground. But after shaking for a while, it fell back to the ground with a loud bang.

“Whoosh…”

Because of the movement of the dragon’s body, a large burst of muddy water was lifted up, sweeping towards the City God and Ji Yuan in a wave, but neither of them dodged. In order to not irritate the black dragon, they did not use any spiritual techniques.

The mud and water that clung to the City God’s body was slowly washed away in the rain. When it came to Ji Yuan’s body, the mud and water quickly separated; however, no one at this moment was in the mood to observe it.

Ji Yuan had not spoken a word since the beginning until now. With his supernatural eyes wide open, he could see more clearly than City God Li. He also understood the pain the black dragon was enduring. There was a strange sense of disharmony about its body.

Seeing how he was struggling, he finally spoke up.

“Black Flood Dragon, you live in one of the four major lakes of Dazhen, under the care of the Dragon Lord. Who would dare to pull out your Dragon Tendons?”

Although Ji Yuan’s voice was strong and peaceful, his words were shocking. City God Li looked at Mr. Ji in horror, and turmoil broke out in his heart.

The black flood dragon gathered up its remaining strength, opened its eyes wide, and looked at the man beside the City God.

Ji Yuan looked up at the dark rain clouds and bursts of lightning in the sky. It seemed as though his sight penetrated the clouds and strong wind. The movements of the black flood dragon’s tendons being pulled must have been considerable, and likely had not happened within the country of Dazhen.

City God Li’s eyes wandered back over to the dragon, finally locking onto the ice crystal at the black flood dragon’s back.

“I’m afraid that I can see the spot where it happened. Since Mr. Ji has spoken, it must be correct. In other words, has the “Lord Mo” of Guangdong Lake truly lost his Dragon Tendons? No wonder his water Qi has been leaking out like crazy!”

Ji Yuan walked slowly across the muddy ground, staring into the black flood dragon’s eyes, and slowly moving towards his head.

The sludge on the ground automatically separated the moment his shoes stepped upon it, leaving only clean water. The City God finally noticed this strange phenomenon, as did the black flood dragon, who was still watching Ji Yuan

Ji Yuan’s supernatural discernment was not omnipotent; he could see some clues, but the conclusion was not obvious. At this time, he bowed solemnly with cupped hands and tentatively spoke.

“My name is Ji Yuan, and I am an old friend of the Dragon Lord of the Tongtian River. Your Dragon Tendons were pulled out, and you are seriously injured. You can’t hold on for much longer. Please tell me what happened, and I will inform the Dragon Lord. If you don’t trust me, I will immediately go and invite the Dragon Lord here.”

“Rooo…”

The black dragon’s eyes moved a few times. It opened its mouth for a long time, but only uttered these sounds.

Now, not only Ji Yuan, but also the City God could feel that something was wrong.

Ji Yuan’s eyes flickered. He approached the black flood dragon carefully. He looked into the other dragon’s eyes and nodded slightly. The pupils in the latter’s amber eyes slowly contracted into vertical slits, allowing Ji Yuan to reach out and touch one of his whiskers.

At this moment, Ji Yuan’s body and mind were in harmony, and his inner world gradually emerged. At a certain moment, he stopped and looked towards the dragon’s eye again. The latter’s pupils slowly opened once more, as if in silent communication with Ji Yuan.

It was also at this moment that Ji Yuan slowly took a few steps back, waved his sleeves, and gathered a large ball of rain in his hands. He kept an Edict in his mouth, but did not utter it. The spiritual energy in his body began to evaporate, and his body seemed to turn into mist and smoke, nearly invisible to the naked eye.

Then he waved his arm again, and a large pool of water and light few out, forming several huge words of glowing water about three or four zhang about the black dragon’s body.

City God Li was no ordinary man. Seeing this scene, he knew something strange was going on. Divine light appeared all over his body, like a rainbow at his back. His thick robe bulged, ready to attack with all his strength at any time.

The Green Vine Sword behind Ji Yuan rose slowly and flew to the top of its master’s head, floating horizontally.

He looked up at the sky, at the continuous sounds and flashes of lightning and thunder. It was the perfect time. Although his method of lightning control was extremely poor, his Edict technique was constantly improving, and he had even researched endless variations.

Seeing that everything was ready, the mysterious yellow energy in Ji Yuan’s mind emerged, and he suddenly issued a command:

“Edict: exorcise and bind the demons!”

Swish…

The four large characters made of water above the dragon’s body instantly transformed from crystal white light to a dazzling golden glow. At the same time, Ji Yuan covered his forehead with his left hand, and a small pit exploded under his feet. With his spiritual energy greatly depleted, he simply broke free with brute force to retreat violently. Seeing this, the City God to the side also jumped back.

“Boom…”

“Boom…”

“Crack…”

“Crack…”

“Crack…”

​​More thunder fell from the sky, converging onto the golden characters. Then the golden characters that had gathered that electric light suddenly pressed down.

“Rooaaar–“

The black flood dragon roared violently. This time, it sounded exactly like a dragon roar.

“Ah…ah…”

As the golden characters embedded themselves into the dragon’s skin, a sharp and inexplicable sound erupted from the black dragon’s body. A dirty shadow continually twisted in the golden light and electricity. The lightning seemed to have become chains, like dancing silver snakes, constantly entwining around its body. .

“Rooooar…”

The black dragon opened its mouth wide as it roared, and a large amount of black ink-like filth spewed out of its maw. The dirty shadows were like smoke, constantly seeping out from the dragon’s nose, eyes, and mouth, gathering again into a distorted shadow above the dragon’s back, firmly bound by electric golden light.

“Execute.”

A peaceful and gentle voice came from Ji Yuan’s mouth, and in the very next moment the Green Vine Immortal Sword was unsheathed.

“Shiiing!”

The sword light flowed with the chime of the sword, and sword energy filled with sword intent slashed across the dragon’s body.

“Aaah…”

The sharp and strange scream stopped abruptly and dissipated into a plume of black smoke.

“Haah…”

Ji Yuan calmed and eased his state, then waved his sleeves, removing the characters that still glowed with electricity from the dragon’s body and putting them back into his sleeves. He couldn’t be too wasteful with his lost spiritual energy and mysterious yellow energy.

Forgot to put this in the last chapter – 双 (shuāng): two/double/pair, 拱桥 (gǒngqiáo): arch bridge

The phonetic the author uses for the dragon cries is 哞 is “Mōu” or “Moo,” hence why he keeps calling it the sound of an old cow. I translated it as “Rooo” because a) it’s half of “Roar” so you know what the sound is, b) it just reads WEIRD to have “dragon goes moo,” and c) it sounds more accurate to what I believe the author was going for

T/n: I know this is supposed to be a serious scene but I can’t help but imagine someone’s cow getting YEETED through the atmosphere, getting a contrail as it passes through the clouds, and creating a cartoonish giant crater

Famous idiom about a “Lord Ye” who was absolutely obsessed with dragons, but when a real dragon decided to honor his devotion by paying him a visit, Lord Ye was so terrified of the real thing that he ran away. Ends with: “what Lord Ye loved was not the real dragon, but something that merely looked like a dragon.”

132-165m / 433-541ft. Approx. a 45-55 story building’s height

Chapter 227: Flooding

As the sword light dissipated, Flood Dragon Mo lay on the ground, gasping for breath. Although his breathing was worse than before, he clearly felt a sense of relief.

The City God’s mentality was just as relaxed as that of the flood dragon on the ground. Although the events just now were very strange, in the end, Mr. Ji was by his side. The feeling of relief from having someone tall to hold up the sky when it fell1 was not only something experienced by the common people.

Looking around the valley of Shuanggongqiao Village, most of the people in the houses had fainted from the dragon’s roar and the sharp piercing screams they had heard at close range. Many of them had blood oozing from their ears.

However, City God Li had acted just in time now, and a rainbow light instantly covered a large area. Most people would be fine. At most, they would suffer some temporary deafness after waking up, and it would not affect their future lives for too long. Of course, short-term panic was inevitable.

Ji Yuan still kept his supernatural eyes open with all his strength, carefully observing the black dragon’s body. At the same time, he also swept his gaze towards the sky, not daring to let his guard down for the time being.

Although his Dharma Eyes were quite miraculous, as the number of things he came to understand and came into contact with grew, the more Ji Yuan understood that some things could not be intuitively discerned even with his supernatural eyes fully open.

Strictly speaking, it was quite difficult to find a way to completely hide from him in this world, but one could hide their clues very well. This so-called insight was also relative; even with the Dharma Eyes, he needed to be extremely careful. He couldn’t rule out the possibility that something had escaped his sight.

After a long time, Ji Yuan breathed a sigh of relief. He figured everything should be fine for the time being.

“Alright, Flood Dragon Mo, are you able to talk about it now?”

“Hooh…hooh…hooh…”

As the black dragon gasped for breath, streams of white vapor gushed out its mouth. Otherwise, the water essence pouring out of its body gradually stopped.

He had originally wanted to use his dragon claws to support himself to stand up, but after struggling for a while, he didn’t have the strength to do so. He could only reluctantly move his dragon head towards Ji Yuan.

“Thank you… Thank you, Mr. Ji, for saving me… Otherwise, even if I, Mo Rong, had died… I’m afraid I would have no peace!”

Mo Jiao’s eyes swiveled, and his gaze swept around. His sight obviously roamed over the surrounding villages, and then over Li Baotian, the City God of Lishun Prefecture, before returning to Ji Yuan.

“Mr. Ji, I hope you might still invite the Dragon Lord to come. My dragon tendons have been pulled out, and my energy has almost dissipated. I don’t have much time left. But I won’t feel at ease unless I tell the Dragon King in person what weighs on my heart.”

City God Li Baotian frowned and spoke in surprise.

“Not even Mr. Ji can handle it?”

Flood Dragon Mo flicked his whiskers, eyes tired.

“Mr. Ji’s words are Edicts that can bind evil, so he is naturally quite powerful… But I still trust the Dragon Lord more… Mr. Ji, I hope you can forgive me!”

As the dragon spoke, he exhaled a fishy stench.

“Haah…hooh…haah…”

After speaking a few words, Flood Dragon Mo seemed to have expended a lot of energy and was out of breath.

“Okay, I will send a letter to the Tongtian River immediately!”

Ji Yuan didn’t mind. He had never been that petty. After all, this was a monster, so it was normal for him not to trust Ji Yuan. After all, he was not one of the dragon’s family members or relatives from the Tongtian River, nor was he like the River God of the Chunmu River who relied on him. Moreover, this was a dragon, and he would have his pride even when dying.

Without any hesitation, Ji Yuan pointed his sword at the sky. The Green Vine Sword soared into the air, breaking through the dark clouds in a few strikes of lightning. It flew straight up, faster and faster, before finally escaping into the strong wind and flying towards the Capital Prefecture.

“Mr. Ji, it may not be appropriate for Flood Dragon Mo’s body to stay in this village. Please, use your spiritual powers to transport him to Guangdong Lake.”

Considering the impact the falling dragon would have on the mortals, City God Li hoped that Ji Yuan could move the giant flood dragon, which was over dozens of zhang long, to Guangdong Lake, which was over thirty li away.

The “transportation” that the City God mentioned naturally did not refer to physically moving the body, but rather a type of technique, collectively known as the Transportation Technique. Among the different Transportation Techniques were methods of commanding ghosts and gods to move, as well as transformation methods of absorbing spiritual energy. Each subcategory had its own advantages and limitations.

But… Ji Yuan’s eyes widened as he looked at this huge creature with a dark body and dense scales. How could someone like him move this?

But it truly wasn’t appropriate for the flood dragon to remain here like this. Once the rain stopped, it would definitely attract onlookers. Moreover, the dragon’s aura and power were so great that if the people observed it for too long, it might scare the timid ones to death.

Thinking of rain, Ji Yuan subconsciously looked at the sky, and thought that this heavy downpour might be of some use.

Without saying much, he flipped his left and right hands alternately, slid his sleeves back, and waved his hands in the air. Under the control of Ji Yuan’s spiritual energy, a large amount of spiritual energy was released along with the technique. At the same time, a chess piece appeared inside his sleeve.

Although Ji Yuan’s Dantian was not large, and the boundary of his spiritual power was not very expansive, the red Qi in his inner world’s alchemy furnace was very powerful. His biggest advantage was that it recovered quickly. As long as the spiritual energy maintained an acceptable rate of consumption, as it was now, he practically had an endless supply of the flow.

As Ji Yuan performed his technique, large tracts of rain began to gather nearby. In many places, they even had the sense that the downpour had dropped from heavy to moderate, or even light, rain.

“Crash shhh… shhhhh…”

City God Li noticed that the water beneath his feet was rising higher and higher. He looked at the houses in the surrounding villages and saw that their base had already flooded. However, although the water level was almost as high as the door, it did not flood into the interior of the houses.

‘What an exquisite water control ability! ’

Unless they had been worshiped as a land god from the very beginning, city gods and other ghosts and gods relied almost solely on the power of incense and faith to practice. They were more adept at using ghost techniques, or “Yin” techniques, and had great limitations in using techniques of the Five Elements. After all, they lacked a physical body. Even if they had succeeded in forging a golden body, they would obviously not practice any five-element techniques as their signature skill. However, that didn’t mean that the City God was unable to distinguish high achievements in such techniques.

As the saying went, just a small glimpse could reveal the larger picture; although it was only one aspect, it was enough for Li Baotian, the City God of Lishun Prefecture, to imagine the ability of a figure like Mr. Ji to use various imperial techniques.

“Crash shhh shhhh… Crash shh shhhh…”

As the water level rose higher and higher, the entire body of the black dragon was soon submerged.

The heavy rain was still falling, but it seemed as though the entire front half of Shuanggongqiao Village had been submerged in a large lake. The waves surged at the entrance to the village, but had no impact on the houses.

Ji Yuan actually used a rather tricky method, because he didn’t know that many techniques; for a long time he only knew a small fire-control technique a minor water-avoidance technique, so he had concerned himself primarily with fine control of techniques for the longest time.

The existence of the sound of the Edicts also allowed him to use various techniques smoothly. Additionally, he pulled out a chess piece and held it in his left sleeve, continuously absorbing the surrounding spiritual energy and the water essence that the black dragon had previously leaked, reducing the loss of his spiritual energy and increasing his efficiency. In this manner, he could control the water at his current level.

However, Ji Yuan quickly discovered a problem at this time. He looked back at the black flood dragon submerged in the water.

‘It seems…it won’t float in the water, but rather sink…’

The body of the flood dragon was too dense and very heavy. It was like a pile of black iron dozens of feet long, motionless in the water.

This was very embarrassing. With Ji Yuan’s current ability to control water, relying on the continuous recovery of Qi to use his spiritual energy, it wasn’t too difficult for him to create waves. However, if he needed the power of a tsunami, this would exceed the limit of his spiritual energy consumption.

To use the words of his previous life, the water could flow continuously, but it lacked a strong explosive power.

Fortunately, this embarrassment didn’t last long. After a few breaths, Flood Dragon Mo, who was originally in a dazed state, seemed to have regained some vitality, and was clearly in better spirits after feeling himself immersed in the water.

Mo Rong could feel the gentleness of the water flow, and he could also feel the abundant spiritual energy and water essence contained in the currents. He could feel his physical strength slowly recovering, and even the pain in his body was significantly alleviated.

“Thank you, Mr. Ji!”

Mo Jiao’s two dragon whiskers danced up and down in the water. A pair of amber dragon eyes opened again, and its four dragon claws slowly kicked backwards. Its entire body twisted in the water like a snake, pushing away waves in the current.

“Crash…”

A large amount of turbid water rose from the newly-created “big lake,” and a huge black dragon swam to the bottom.

Ji Yuan let out a sigh of relief. At the same time, he controlled the wind to continue blowing, causing the water to flow and form a gentle cresting wave, flowing all the way back towards Guangdong Lake.

The village was 30 li away from Guangdong Lake, quite an ordinary distance. For City God Li Baotian, Ji Yuan, or Mo Jiao, this distance couldn’t even be considered a walk. Now, it was a small “Long March.”2

The City God of Lishun Prefecture was still a little puzzled at first. Mr. Ji used such a an exquisite water control technique to help the dying black flood dragon swim back to Guangdong Lake. The amount of energy and spiritual power spent in the process was incalculable. It would be far more convenient to directly transport the dragon to Guangdong Lake.

But slowly, City God Li understood the deeper meaning. Flood Dragon Mo went from struggling in the waves to gradually swimming smoothly. Its energy and spirit had recovered, and water essence was no longer simply leaking out. In particular, the recovery of its “spirit” was an incredibly rare achievement.

‘As his legends say, Mr. Ji respects and values every person who is determined to seek the Tao and practice diligently.’

The City God concluded that because the black flood dragon was swimming at this moment, he had not only recovered some of his physical strength, but also a kind of confidence, going from not being able to stand up to being able to swim on his own. His heart was strong, as was his spirit, which could at least help him hold on for a while longer.

Shuanggongqiao Village was actually surrounded by a small creek, with an arch bridge in front and one at the back of the village, hence its name.3 At this moment, Ji Yuan was controlling the water around the flood dragon, and the two sides worked together to control the waves to pass the arch bridge at the front of the village. Then they entered the river gully.

However, the gully was too small to accommodate the dragon, so waves of water six to seven zhang high still followed it, spreading more than 10 zhang to either size of the gully. To some extent, it seemed as if a mighty wall of water was constantly advancing.

The creek was connected Guangdong Lake. The journey of more than 30 li would pass through some villages and towns. Some people in the surrounding towns saw large waves of water to either side of the gully in the heavy rain, rushing towards their side of the river. Many people shouted in panic.

“It’s flooding… it’s flooding!”

“It’s a flood!”

“Oh my goodness, how did the water in the creek get so high?”

But the panic only lasted for a short while, and soon people discovered that this tidal wave of flooding just rolled forwards along the small gully, as if a god had suddenly poured water out of a bucket. It did not cause much impact to the place where people lived to either side of the riverbank. The little layer of water that rolled over them didn’t even come to above their ankles.

At the same time, there were also some living on higher ground, watching this strange flood from a distance. They could see that the center was turbid, and it looked as though some huge monster was stirring the water and advancing.

From Shuanggongqiao Village to Guangdong Lake, the whole journey of over 30 li took more than an hour. The total amount of spiritual energy Ji Yuan consumed was irrelevant; more important was the intense consumption of his mental energy. Even the way he gathered spiritual energy and collected the water essence scattered by the dragon while carefully controlling the “large wave” to move forward was incredible.

After the flood receded, the gully had been widened several times, and its shape in many places resembled the tracks of a winding snake. At the starting point, Shuanggouqiao Village, there was a thick and deep mark of something crawling on the ground in the large valley field, extending all the way to the gully.

T/n: There’s something deeply funny about JY describing their movements as a “Long March” with the connotations of guerilla warfare and intense strategizing, and then JY and the dragon just bulldozing down the creek with a giant wall of water (I’m thinking Interstellar mega wave style), and people going “yep, this is normal”

i.e. having someone more experienced/skilled take the lead

长征 (Chángzhēng): expedition/long journey BUT SPECIFICALLY refers to the “Long March,” a military retreat by the Chinese Red Army (Chinese Communist Party) from advancing Nationalist forces (Kuomintang Party) during the Chinese Civil War in 1934-1935. The retreat took over a year, employing guerilla tactics to avoid blockades and ambushes and brought a lot of prestige to the Communist Party. In this case, they’re using the example in the story to allude to prolonged use of stealth tactics to avoid mundane notice as they move a ginormous dragon 15km/~10miles
